38
THEOPHR. PARA.
<meta>catchword: lisq;</meta>

<margin>Note here: this treatise seems to have been separated from the books on meteorology by Theophrastus Paracelsus.</margin> ...to be concluded, that all impressions have their origin from the stars and the elements, as will be explained later. In these meteorological matters <note>original: *meteoricis*</note>, however, we shall relate by what reasoning such impressions are born from them, and how it came to pass that other writers on meteors—having no share in our opinion and philosophy—nevertheless describe their own dreams as meteorological phenomena. Perhaps this is because the spirits <note>Paracelsus often refers to "spirits" as the active, invisible forces or "seeds" within matter that drive natural processes.</note> fixed those meteorological things which they write about; yet, while casting their opinions toward the goal, they did not reach it. 

It is not proper for us to follow or value the devised subtleties of some fictional spirit as if they were philosophically sufficient, unless nature alone displays the true spirit—that is, its true foundation based on certain experience <note>original: *experientia*; for Paracelsus, "experience" meant direct, hands-on observation of nature and chemical processes, rather than just reading old books.</note>. Otherwise, no philosopher arises, for the philosopher is produced in and from nature and is proven through experience, not in the sharp wits of fantasy <note>original: *spiritibus acutis phantasiæ*</note>, which take delight in their own imaginings. Whoever desires to be a natural philosopher <term>physicus</term> <note>In the 16th century, a *physicus* was a student of nature or a physician, rather than a "physicist" in the modern sense.</note> must join us in faithful observation, so that he may consider how incorporeal spirits deal with us no differently than masters deal with their servants and subjects.

<summary>Paracelsus argues that true meteorological knowledge must be rooted in the observation of nature and the elements rather than the "fantasies" of previous writers. He asserts that a true philosopher is formed by experience and understands the relationship between invisible spirits and the physical world.</summary>

### ON PREMONITIONS
<meta>catchword: mus</meta>

...and their own, which ought to be understood in this way. Whatever the masters themselves command, the servants and subjects are compelled to execute; and for this reason, servants can be considered nothing other than the fools <note>original: *stulti*; in the 16th century, a 'fool' or jester was a dependent in a noble court. Paracelsus uses this to describe those who lack intellectual and spiritual independence.</note> of their masters. For each person could be a master in their own right, rather than the fool of some other master; but where there is submission, it is necessary that fools also be present.

In no different way, incorporeal **spirits** <term>spiritus</term> <note>In Paracelsian thought, *spiritus* are invisible forces or entities that inhabit the elements and can influence human thought and the physical world.</note> exalt themselves and their dominion over us. By that very act, we soon become fools; that is, we devise a feigned wisdom out of our own **fantasy** <note>original: *phantasia*; Paracelsus uses this term to describe groundless speculation or purely mental constructs that have no basis in the physical reality of nature.</note>. We then employ this "wisdom," using art and logical reasonings, while ignoring all **experience**—which nevertheless ought to be the mother of reason, wisdom, and science.

Therefore, for the reasons stated above, we are the "fools" of the spirits. They play jokes on us just as masters do with their fools, and the spirits speak whatever they wish through them. Those who seek their **wisdom** <term>sapientia</term> <note>True wisdom, for the author, is the divine understanding granted to those who study the "Book of Nature" directly.</note> from experience are to be praised and considered truly wise; conversely, those who hold themselves in high esteem and make themselves out to be wise are to be blamed, for the spirits rule over them as if they were their own fools. From these things we can recognize that we have by no means been in an oven or in a pot <note>This likely refers to alchemical vessels, suggesting that these mental "impressions" do not come from a controlled laboratory environment but from external spiritual influences.</note> from which we have seen impressions...

<vocab>spirits, fantasy, wisdom, experience, fools, masters, servants</vocab>

<summary>Paracelsus argues that humans become "fools" to spiritual influences when they rely on speculative fantasy rather than direct experience of the natural world. He asserts that true wisdom must be birthed from experience, which he calls the "mother" of all science and reason.</summary>

<keywords>Paracelsus, spirits, fantasy, experience, wisdom, master and servant, natural philosophy, scholasticism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on wisdom and experience. ->THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS<-

...[we] can. Is it therefore necessary to change our opinion? It is so that we might proceed from one region into another, in which we may learn about the **inferior nature**. Through certain and true experience of this nature—as if in a mirror—we might see the essence and properties of the **superior nature** <note>original: *naturæ superioris*; Paracelsus is applying the Hermetic principle of "As above, so below," suggesting that studying the physical, "inferior" world reveals the truths of the spiritual, "superior" world.</note> and, having been taught, come to know them.

For this reason, we shall rightly pass from one experience to another (since inferior things demonstrate superior ones), and we shall write down all **impressions** according to their origins, one after another, as follows.

### CHAPTER TWO

<margin>The place of the elements.</margin>
We have stated further that God, as God, created the **elements** <term>elementa</term> <note>In this context, elements are not just earth, water, air, and fire, but the fundamental "matrices" or building blocks of the universe.</note> out of nothing. Now we shall philosophize on where He placed that which previously occupied their location, and which [newly] occupied this place. In relation to this, we shall describe the element of **air** more broadly, specifically the place where the aforementioned elements were situated. Furthermore, that which was driven out from there took up its place in **hell** <term>infernum</term> <note>original: *infernum*; here Paracelsus uses the term to describe a lower cosmological region or a state of being "cast out" from the divine order, rather than just a place of punishment.</note> along with all that adhered to it. For it is **heaven** <term>coelum</term> <note>The celestial realm or the firmament.</note> from which it was cast down, and it is the place in which the elements are now contained. That which was not driven from that place into hell is in the very place which [is] prefer... <note>The text breaks off here; "præser" is likely the start of "præsertim" (especially) or "præservatur" (is preserved).</note>

# ON PRESAGES
<note>original: *DE PRAESAGI.*; likely a truncated form of *De Praesagiis* (On Omens or Forewarnings), a common theme in Paracelsian thought regarding how the physical world reflects divine or spiritual shifts.</note>

<margin>Paradise</margin>
...[con]served for the sake of incorruptibility; this is **Paradise**. The other place belongs to the **elements** <term>elementa</term> <note>In Paracelsian philosophy, elements are the four physical building blocks—Earth, Water, Air, and Fire—which are "corruptible," meaning they change and decay.</note>. These elements have now been essentially made and completed within the circuit of the sphere, so that there is no longer any place there that can exist as celestial or infernal—unless perhaps there were something in that place where nothing is human or can be human. In such a place, **hell** <term>infernus</term> might be enclosed, and there lie those who were cast down; or perhaps they must exist beneath us, stripped of all power of joy; or they were cast out and bound into the very substance of the elements.

<margin>What hell is.</margin>
For this is the greatest hell: to live and linger within that which is transitory <note>original: *in transibili*; referring to the physical world where everything eventually dies or changes.</note>, and not to exist as they once did in their own kingdom. Indeed, it must be considered hell when an opted angel is driven from Paradise and deprived of it. There can be no greater hell than this: that he who is eternal, and has nothing of decay within himself, should be forced to dwell within the corruptible and the transitory. He cannot suffer a greater punishment or torment than this.

We conclude, therefore, that the place of the four elements was originally the place of **heaven** from which **Lucifer** was cast down. Nothing was driven out from that place except him and his followers. Only this corruptible heaven remained, and it was set into the elements. These...

...his punishment (namely Lucifer's) is that he must remain in it (that is, in the corruptible [world]). He did not previously think that God would place such a heaven and delightful place into the **elements** <term>elementa</term> <note>Paracelsus views the elements—Earth, Water, Air, and Fire—not just as physical matter, but as the "prison" or "garment" of the fallen spirits.</note>, for he had intended to remain in heaven forever, and for this reason, he believed nothing would be lacking to him. 

Therefore, when God created the elements—while Lucifer and the others were unaware—at that same instant, that world was created and the fallen one was cast down into "hell." By this, it is meant he was cast out from the light of heaven into the darkness—that is, into the **corruptible world** <note>original: *mundum corruptibile*; in alchemy and early science, "corruptible" means subject to decay, change, and death, unlike the eternal heavens.</note>. 

For this reason, a portion of those who are his companions dwell in **fire**, a portion in the **air**, a portion in the **earth**, and others indeed in the **water**. No devil dwells outside of the elements; rather, they all live within them. When the end of the world is reached, there shall be one fold for all of those who are enclosed within the four elements. 

Thus, the beginning of the four elements was created out of nothing <note>original: *ex nihilo*</note>, for the place in which they now exist previously contained nothing corruptible from which a corruptible thing could be made. ### CHAPTER THREE

It should be known that in this place, the element of air encloses and sustains the other elements, and even all creatures, so that they may remain in the order in which they were created. Since the element of air was so created that it contains the other three under its power, and sustains them no differently than a foundation sustains a house; they are placed within it so that it may hold and carry them.

# THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS

...concludes and holds the matter <note>Original: *rem concludit ac tenet*. This continues the thought that the air acts as a container for the other elements.</note> so that it cannot give way from its place, nor even break out. For it would be impossible for that which is corruptible and destined to perish to fall away into eternity; indeed, it cannot fall, for the reason that all things are oriented upward and not downward, and they have no bottom into whose depth they might fall. <margin>The concavity is the foundation of the eternal air.</margin> The air is so powerfully compacted and preserved within a sphere <term>sphaera</term> <note>In Paracelsian cosmology, the "sphere" refers to the firmament or the outer boundary of the created world.</note> that it is no more possible for it to break out or fail than for a kingdom situated outside to be able to perish; yet, since it ought to perish and fail, there is no other failing in this place than that everything ought to fall into the center—and fall it shall. That is, the air and the stars will fall into the globule <term>globulum</term> <note>The "globule" refers to the Earth, the heavy center of the universe.</note>, which will be consumed by them so that no ash shall remain in the future. Therefore, there is a transitory corruption and end—namely of the heavens and of all elements—such that nothing of them falls outside, but everything falls inward and into the center. This is the highest of all the secret mysteries of philosophy: namely, that such an immense sphere should fall entirely into the center. This is because no depth exists on the outside into which it could fall, but only on the inside, and because in that...

...so that nothing is placed downward from the outside, but truly downward from the inside, and all things do not fall upward: <note>original: *sursum decidant*. ### CHAPTER FOUR

You have heard <note>original: *adiuistis*, likely a typo for *audivistis*</note> that the air sustains all the elements and all creatures <term>creaturae</term> <note>In this context, *creaturae* refers to all physical things created by God, not just living animals.</note> so that they may remain in their course and in the center—that is, so they may persevere in the circle and the center. In this way, surely the earth and the elements are the very middle of all created things, just like the center of a circle from which the element of air proceeds. These are round in themselves, like a sphere, which would be drawn back into the center unless it were sustained by something. Therefore, the earth and the sea are one little globe <term>globulus</term> and one sphere, which rests upon no physical thing, but is free on all sides except for its binding to the other. For this reason, the philosopher will not wonder why it does not fall or break; nor will the natural scientist <term>Physicus</term> <note>A scholar of *physica*, the study of the natural world and its laws.</note> think it is some light thing flying in the air, because it is sustained by God in a wondrous way. This little globe is not thus two... <margin>The Earth is sustained by God.</margin>

...of those elements. This is nothing other than pure air of a thick and visible substance, like the most excellent Chaos <note>original: *Chaos*. In Paracelsian philosophy, Chaos refers to the atmospheric "Great Mystery" or the primordial void that contains the elements and sustains life.</note>, through which nothing can be seen. Those who are inexperienced have called this "heaven." 

This Chaos is the elemental tree from which air goes out into all creatures. It is round, and it surrounds the entire little globe <term>globulus</term> or sphere, enclosing everything that is comprised of the other three elements. Furthermore, outside of Chaos there is no longer any creature that is subject to the elements, but within Chaos itself all elemental creatures <term>elementatæ creaturæ</term> <note>These are beings or things composed of the four elements: earth, water, air, and fire.</note> are included. Such a Chaos contains the earth itself and the sea, ensuring they remain in their center and do not move away from it. 

You should understand it in this way: Just as the yolk of an egg <note>original: *vitellus oui*</note> touches the shell at no point because of its own "Chaos"—that is, the egg white <note>original: *albumen*</note> which lies all around it—so also between these elements, namely the air and the earth, there is a clear medium in which the sphere of the little globe is sustained, so that it remains unmoved, just as the yolk...

in the egg, which in no part departs from its own center. Although that which supports the little globe of water <note>original: *aquæ globulum*. Paracelsus describes the Earth and its oceans as a singular suspended sphere.</note> is not visible—unlike the white of an egg, which is both sensible and visible—you may consider the cause of this to be that the element is born from **Chaos**. This Chaos <note>In the Paracelsian system, "Chaos" refers to the atmosphere or the "Great Mystery" (Mysterium Magnum) that fills the space between the heavens and the earth.</note> persists in its own order under the form of the element of air, etc. That is, it is supported by the **ether** <note>original: *æthere*. It is this active power that supports the said sphere for as long as the air-Chaos remains in God’s power. Otherwise, the Chaos would find an exit, as was stated above, into the center; from this would follow the disruption of the elements <term>disruptio elementorum</term> <note>The total breakdown of the physical laws holding the universe together.</note> and the consumption of all things, and for no other reason. As long as this element remains whole, the clarity will also remain whole, so that nothing falls. And thus the sphere of the globe stands in the middle of the element of air, as the center of it...

of the sphere. Whoever wishes to philosophize more broadly on why all creatures are standing upright—so much so that they all think they exist above others, and imagine those others to be beneath them or to their side, based on a simple consideration of the little globe—the cause is this: all things are directed in a straight line from the center to the concavity of the sphere. Just as in a circle many thousands of lines can be seen drawn from the center to the circumference, or as one may see in a wheel, in no other way are all growing plants and animals erected toward heaven in every direction. Thus, whatever might be upon the sphere of the globe is itself on a line turned toward the **Chaos** <note>In Paracelsian cosmology, Chaos is the invisible air or atmosphere that fills the space between the Earth and the firmament.</note>, and it has no other support to keep it from falling than the **light** <note>original: *clarum*. This refers to the subtle, clear substance or ether that provides the structural integrity of the heavens.</note>. To the creatures themselves, all other things seem to be situated to the side or below them. 

However, the fact that these positions do not actually exist as they appear is the work and creation of God. Those who are on the opposite side of the globe <note>Paracelsus is here describing the concept of the Antipodes—people on the other side of the world.</note> seem to hang with their heads downward, and yet they do not fall. But in truth, for them, that position is not "downward," but "upward"; even within their own bodies, their active powers <term>operationes</term> <note>The functional or vital forces that drive biological and chemical processes.</note> act in an upward direction, and they stand in nothingness <note>original: *in nihilo*. Referring to the void or the seemingly empty space of the atmosphere.</note>. This is a singular work of God, which should not be over-philosophized, and by whom it has been so ordained that all things occur "upward" in all their functions. <meta>This page continues the discussion on cosmology and physics, moving from the orientation of creatures on a spherical earth to a critique of solar attraction and the nature of the atmosphere and heavens.</meta>

ON FOREKNOWLEDGE <note>original: *DE PRAESAG.*, likely an abbreviation for *De Praesagiis*, referring to the study of signs or foreknowledge in nature.</note>
d 4

And it is an overly crude philosophy that teaches that the sun draws all things to itself, and that whatever it attracts stands upright; there are many improbable things here, and many forgotten things that have no foundation in philosophy. For the stars <note>original: *astra*</note> have no such power of drawing, on account of their suspension from the firmament. We marvel, however, at those who wish to defend this reasoning; for how would it then happen that the stars, as they grow, do not draw animal creatures directly to themselves, and what could they [the animals] provide that would be able to resist such a pull? But indeed, the spirit is accustomed to drive its own foolish followers everywhere.

> That the heaven does not revolve, and yet
> It boils with the element of fire,
> And gives forth **Chaos** and air.

### CHAPTER FIVE

Again, let us philosophize concerning the element of air—what moves within it, or what it is that moves it, and whether it is moved or not. This is the resolution: Some think that the air—that is, the heaven—is revolved, which is not at all true, that it is driven perpetually in a circle. For this is not the place of its revolution,

<note>original vocabulary: philosophy, stars, firmament, element of air, heaven, to be revolved</note>

but of remaining quietly and fixedly in one place. However, the air emerging from it into the world—that is, the white or the clarity <note>original: *albumen aut clarum*; Paracelsus frequently compares the structure of the cosmos to an egg, where the "albumen" or egg-white represents the atmosphere.</note> which sustains it—is taken from its own **Chaos** <note>original: *Chaos*; in Paracelsian physics, this refers to the atmospheric "void" or the source of the life-giving breath/air.</note>, that is, from the circle of the element of air. This vapor presents air to us in the world and is the clarity or "white" for sustaining the center.

But so that you may distinguish one philosophy from the other regarding the motion or rest of the heaven, know this: If the heaven were driven in a circle, so that this circulation subsequently carried the air in the world along with it (which is not the case), what clarity would it provide upon which the world and the sea were founded? Therefore, it is a certain wind that causes the course, and not the air. Moreover, it is necessary and without harm that this wind should rest; for when it rests in its own discharge, it presents a uniform air such as the world must have, being neither less nor greater in one place than in another. For in its roundness it contributes a uniform operation toward the center. For when

<margin>d 5</margin>

...for if a man moves from the highest part of the center to the sides—where there might otherwise be less air—he possesses just as much as he does above the center, where the greatest amount of air would exist. You should understand it in this way: Since the center is located in the middle (that is, the world and the sea), if the heaven were then to run around and about that center, the wind descending from the heaven to the earth would bring about an inequality. For the right sides <note>original: "Sgen"; possibly a printer's abbreviation or a specific directional term denoting the orientation of the celestial sphere.</note> would have less air than the left side of the revolution; thus, the right sides would be suffocated by a lack of air, while those along the line of the leftward course would have an excess of heavy air. This is clearly impossible; instead, it happens in the following manner: 

<insert>When Chaos</insert> boils and exerts pressure, it drives out a vapor from itself like a boiling pot. This <term>vapor</term> moves toward the center along a line—that is, along any perpendicular line that converges upon the center. In this way, an equal amount of vapor exits from <term>Chaos</term> <note>In Paracelsian thought, "Chaos" is the atmospheric void or "great mystery" from which the elements are nourished.</note> into the world; that is, a clear <term>wind</term> arises solely from the <term>boiling</term> <note>original: "ebullitio"; the metaphorical "seething" or "bubbling" of the elemental source which produces the atmosphere.</note> that it produces. This kind of boiling of the vapor does not proceed from the specific form, strength, or power of the Chaos itself, as if it could boil purely of its own accord; rather, it occurs because one element adheres to another, and one without...

<meta>Catchword: without (absque)</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus argues that the air is distributed equally around the Earth because it "boils" out of the surrounding Chaos like steam from a pot, rather than being dragged around by a rotating sky, which would cause an uneven and suffocating distribution.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Paracelsus’s explanation of atmospheric physics. Having established that the air "boils" out of Chaos, he now identifies the "element of fire" (the stars and firmament) as the heat source that drives this process, ensuring the air is generated uniformly for human respiration.</meta>

->THEOPHRastus PARACelsus<-

...[without another it] cannot persist. For the nearest element lying beneath the air—that is, the <term>star</term> <note>original: "astrum"; in Paracelsian physics, this refers to the collective celestial bodies or the "astral" influence.</note> and the firmament—causes the celestial <term>Chaos</term> to boil equally by its own fire, which acts as an element. It does this not more on one side than on another—as if it would boil more fervently and emit more air in places where there are more stars—but rather like a pot exposed to four fires, it boils only as one, and does so equally. Likewise, the distribution of the element of fire exists not only for the sake of igniting or causing things to boil for the sake of beauty, but out of the necessity of the operations that proceed from it. Therefore, the element of fire is the cause of the boiling within the Chaos itself, from which the <term>vapor</term> proceeds—that is, the <term>clear white</term> <note>original: "albumen clarum"; Paracelsus often used the anatomy of an egg as a cosmogenic model, where the air corresponds to the egg white.</note> and the air which carries the world at its center, and gives the breath of respiration to man, as the region in which man must live.

...heaven, not considering that this space is <term>corruptible</term> and transitory, which in no way befits the true, eternal heaven. Although this whiteness does not appear as it truly is, it looks like a green or azure color depending on the quality of the eyes, because of the "smoke" of the stars, the vapor exhaled by them, and the distance between the Chaos and ourselves. These factors change the color according to their conditions, so that the smoke of their <term>salt</term> <note>Paracelsus believed the stars emitted a spiritualized "salt" or vapor that filled the atmosphere.</note> prevails as a dark azure and mostly greenish color. Thus, a particular Chaos exists between the true Chaos and us, appearing in azure and green colors according to the clouds that are placed in between; these create the azure color in the sky. ### CHAPTER SEVEN

This is true and well-founded philosophy when we say there are only two elements, namely the **hot** and the **cold**. Under or alongside the hot, the "dry" is always understood; and with the cold, the "moist" is understood. Thus, a single piece of matter is understood through two <term>virtues</term> <note>In this context, "virtues" refers to the inherent powers, qualities, or active properties of a substance.</note>. That philosopher is ignorant and unrefined who speaks of four separate elements—hot, dry, cold, and moist—as if four distinct elements stood on their own, which is not true at all. Instead, there are only two; for moist and hot are not found together [as separate foundations], nor are cold and dry. Their relationship is like that of a wife and a husband: these two only...

<summary>Paracelsus concludes that the sky's color is a product of its elemental makeup (the three primes) rather than its true nature. He then argues that the traditional "four elements" are actually just two pairs: hot-dry and cold-moist.</summary>

<keywords>Paracelsus, Chaos, Three Primes, Tria Prima, Aristotelian physics, Hot and Cold, Moist and Dry, Elemental Theory, Philosophy of Nature</keywords>

--- Page 77 ---
<meta>This page continues the analogy from the previous page, where Paracelsus compared the union of elemental qualities to a marriage. He uses this to explain why he groups the four traditional elements into two primary pairs (Fire/Air and Earth/Water), moving away from the complex Galenic theories of "complexions" toward a more functional understanding of nature's cycles.</meta>

<page-num>55</page-num>

->ON PRESAGES<-

...one [is] the man, and the other person is not a man by themselves, but both together make a man, and they remain in one humanity—or rather, in one body. It is no different with the elements: fire and air are one, and likewise earth and water are one. <margin>The whole man.</margin>

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;It is of no use at all to philosophize about the <term>complexions</term> <note>original: "complexionibus"; in traditional medicine, this referred to the specific balance of the four qualities (hot, cold, moist, dry) that determined a person's health or an object's nature. Paracelsus is arguing that these categories are over-complicated.</note> of the elements. It would be better if we, philosophizing in this way, were to say that fire and air have no [separate] complexion, nor do earth and water. Instead, the heat of fire is its "fruit," from which creatures live, and the same is true for air; likewise, the coldness of the earth is also its "fruit" for the use of creatures. 

And so, that which fire has dried out during the summer is itself moistened again during the winter toward the next <term>harvest</term> <note>original: "frugalitatem"; here referring to the fruitfulness or productivity of the earth.</note>. No other philosophy is useful, as will be discussed more extensively regarding the "hot" and the "cold" in its proper place later. Although a greater or lesser degree of heat and cold are found within the <term>globule</term> <note>original: "globulo"; likely referring to the Earth or the terrestrial sphere.</note>, nevertheless one must correspond to the other, and one must prepare the other. Therefore, we should not discourse more broadly from philosophy regarding complexions, but only regarding the <term>house</term> <note>original: "domo"; Paracelsus often uses the "house" as a metaphor for the fundamental structure or environment of the natural world.</note> in which all philosophers rejoice and flourish.

<meta>Catchword: This element</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus argues that the elements should be viewed as functional pairs (Fire-Air and Earth-Water) rather than through complex theoretical "complexions." He views qualities like heat and cold as the "fruits" of these elements which sustain life and balance the seasons.</summary>

<page-num>56</page-num>

### THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS ###

...it is said to be that which has the power of cooling or heating; for the **Heavens** <note>original: "cœlum"; here referring to the upper elements of Fire and Air as a single celestial unit.</note> are a single element, though twofold, and the **Earth** is one element, also twofold. But this philosophy is recognized and proven by the fact that distinct **fruits** <note>In Paracelsian thought, a "fruit" is the specific result or output produced by an element, such as a plant from the earth or a fish from water.</note> are born from them, and in each of them there are two particular ones—in water and in earth. For this reason, they are understood to be two elements, but only in their fruit, and not in their [underlying] nature.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;From this, two books are drawn out, and they are founded upon two elements. Just as from a husband and wife there is only one human being, yet from them two fruits are born, as is shown by their generation. Therefore, since two fruits come forth from the two elements of water and earth, they are rightly recognized as elements; for those things which provide fruits to creatures for their life, and what they require, are elements. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;For this reason, the Heavens are two elements, and the Earth likewise is two. The greater of them is understood as the **male**, and the other, lesser one as the **female** or wife, yet both remain in one <term>complexion</term> <note>original: "complexione"; the fundamental balance of qualities. Paracelsus argues that despite their differences, the paired elements function as a single harmonious unit.</note>. For all these things are fruits and products: just as various kinds of fish are produced from the water, and rain from the **stars** <note>original: "stellis"; Paracelsus often attributed weather patterns like rain to celestial or "astral" influences rather than just atmospheric ones.</note>, and likewise vegetables from the earth.

<meta>catchword: thus</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus explains that while the Heavens and Earth are each a single "nature," they are twofold in function, acting like a husband and wife to produce life. He defines elements not by their substance, but by the "fruits" they provide to sustain living creatures.</summary>

For in the elements, no hot thing is found to be moist, and no cold thing is dry <note>Here Paracelsus rejects the traditional Aristotelian system, which taught that each element was a combination of two qualities (e.g., Fire is hot and dry, Air is hot and moist). &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Thus, each element has its own companion, with which it is made complete, not unlike the eye which is perfected by sight. So also is heat perfected by dryness; as long as moisture is present, no heat can exist there. And just as the eye is not without sight, and one cannot hear through the eye nor see through the ears, it is no more possible for coldness to be dry or heat to be moist. In these matters the ancient philosophers erred, for they placed "hot" and "moist" together in a single element, which is impossible for an element. It can well...

...they judged and commanded that one should stop and go no further, and so on. Therefore, **Astronomy** has five branches <note>original: "species"; here used to mean categories or divisions of the science</note> under it, and each branch has its own particular **Astrology**, such as the **Astrology of Olympus** or the **firmament** <note>referring to the physical heavens and the movements of the celestial bodies</note>, the **Astrology of magic**, the **Astrology of divinations**, the **Astrology of necromancy** <term>necromancy</term> <note>original: "nigromantiæ"; in the 16th century, this often referred to the study of hidden influences, spirits, or the "dead" forces of nature rather than just modern "black magic"</note>, and the **Astrology of nature**.

means they are found together in a single figure <note>original: "figuram"; likely referring to a combined conceptual model or a schematic representation of the heavens</note> and ought to be arranged; what each one is in its operations and powers; and finally, their proofs. Through these proofs, I will demonstrate that these are lawful, true, and natural arts—not deceptive or erroneous, nor "against nature" as the ignorant claim. Therefore, so that they may be supported, I shall set down the orders, definitions, effects, and proofs as follows. The principle and foundation of all these is that which we established above: namely, the perfect knowledge of the five branches <note>original: "specierum"</note>, without which **Astronomy** absolutely does not and cannot exist.

### DEFINITIONS OF THE
### branches of Astronomy.

**Natural Astrology**, which is called both human and ethereal, proceeds from the **firmament**. In its essence, it was created for the sake of knowledge and granted to the **microcosm** <term>microcosm</term> <note>original: "microcosmo"; the philosophical concept that the human being is a "little world" that perfectly mirrors the structures and forces of the great world or universe (the macrocosm)</note>. The **Astrology of magic** and the celestial mansion...

<summary>Paracelsus defends his five branches of Astronomy as natural and lawful sciences, refuting critics who label them "against nature." He introduces Natural Astrology as a discipline originating from the firmament and inherently connected to the human microcosm.</summary>

<page-num>62</page-num>

->THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS<-

...[imagination] <note>The text begins mid-word, likely completing "imaginationem" from the previous page.</note> has its essential nature among the celestial things in heaven: it is granted to those who are reborn, derives its origin from Christ, and is practiced and fulfilled by them. The **Astrology of the New Olympus** <note>original: "Astrologia olympi novi"; Paracelsus uses "Olympus" to refer to the spiritual or ethereal sky, distinguishing it from the physical stars.</note> is derived from those things which the natural heaven is capable of, and beyond; and this is granted to the faithful through faith, and is also fulfilled by them.

The **Astrology of Divinations** <note>original: "Astrologia divinationum"</note> derives its origin from the signs and indicators of natural things, and it fails only in that it possesses little experience; hence the art is uncertain, sometimes true, but most often false.

The **Astrology of Necromancy** <note>original: "Astrologia nigromantiae"</note>, which is called Satanic and of the underworld, arises from the natural aspects of the firmament, and is fulfilled only through infernal spirits. These are "natural Astronomers" who know the spirits perfectly, and they manifest them either through themselves or through human beings.

## THE SUBDIVISION OF
### human Astronomy.

**Human Astronomy** contains seven branches <note>original: "species"</note>.

The first is **Astrology**, which again has three branches: namely, the **Figure of the Heavens**...

<page-num>63</page-num>
ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCE 63

...[of the hea]vens <note>Completing "Coeli" from the previous page's catchword.</note>, the Properties of the stars, and the Particular Mode.

The **Second**, which is also called **Magic**, has five branches under it, such as Images, Gammahey <term>talismanic stones or objects engraved with celestial figures</term>, Likenesses, Words, and Herbs.

The **Third** is **Divination**, of which four branches exist, such as Dreams, Animals <note>original: "Bruta"; referring to divination by observing the behavior of unreasoning beasts.</note>, the Spirit <note>original: "Animus"</note>, and Speculation.

The **Fourth** is **Necromancy**. Its branches are also four, namely **Beryllistics** <term>the art of crystal-gazing or divining through stones</term>, Fates, Visions, and Phantasms.

The **Fifth** is the **Signed** <note>original: "Signatum"; the study of the "signatures" or signs in nature</note>, of which three branches exist, such as **Physiognomy** <term>reading character from facial features</term>, **Chiromancy** <term>palmistry</term>, and the **External Form** <note>original: "Habitus"</note>, which is also called **Proportion**.

The **Sixth** are the **Uncertain Arts**, and these are four, such as **Geomancy** <term>divination by earth or dots on the ground</term>, **Hydromancy** <term>divination by water</term>, **Pyromancy** <term>divination by fire</term>, and **Austromancy** <term>divination by the winds</term>, to which **Imagination** is added.

The **Seventh** is **Manual** <note>Practical or technical astronomy</note>. And of this five branches are found, such as Globes, Instruments, Geography, Cosmography, and Mathematics.

->*Definitions of these branches.*<-

The **Figure of the Heavens** is the art which teaches how to truly recognize in what way the heaven is positioned in its aspects, etc., at any given minute, and the entire firmament.

<page-num>64</page-num>
## THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS

**Property** teaches one to recognize the conditions, properties, and nature of the stars.

The **Particular Mode** teaches the course of the heavens in regard to a single question only; it is effective for a complete judgment and for providing counsel.

**Images** are figures made of metal or wax in which celestial virtues <note>In this context, "virtues" refers to the inherent powers or qualities of the stars acting upon earthly materials.</note> operate.

**Gammahey** <term>talismanic stones or objects engraved with celestial figures</term> are stones impressed and prepared by celestial constellations.

**Likenesses** are celestial figures that exist within things, possessing a similarity to the objects they signify.

**Words** are those things which, whether written or spoken, possess their own virtues and powers.

**Herbs** are those which, beyond their own natural properties, possess celestial powers.

**Dreams** are operations occurring through the stars during sleep.

**Animals** <note>original: "Bruta"; referring to unreasoning beasts</note> are the powers which celestial influences reveal through unreasoning animals.

The **Spirit** <note>original: "Animus"</note> consists of celestial operations occurring within the minds of men.

**Speculation** refers to that which heaven works within one who contemplates or observes something.

**Beryllistics** <term>the art of crystal-gazing or divining through stones</term> are the arts of crystals, fingernails <note>original: "unguium"; the practice of onychomancy, or divining by inspecting reflections on polished fingernails</note>, mirrors, and so on.

<meta>catchword: Fates</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus defines the various sub-categories of astronomical and magical arts, explaining how celestial influences manifest in physical objects like talismans and herbs, as well as in human experiences like dreams and spirit.</summary>

<keywords>Paracelsus, Property, Particular Mode, Images, Gammahey, Likenesses, Words, Herbs, Dreams, Bruta, Animus, Speculation, Beryllistics, Onychomancy, Talismans</keywords>

### ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCES

**Fates** <note>original: "Fata"</note> are certain unexpected operations, occurring beyond the common and natural course of things.

**Visions** are the appearances of spirits, or specters <note>original: "spectra"; refers to ghosts or spirit-forms</note> when they are seen.

**Phantasms** <term>mental images or spirit-forms that appear to have physical presence</term> are generations made and born without human involvement, which nonetheless are regarded as humans and walk about.

**Chiromancy** <term>the art of palmistry; divining character or fate from the lines of the hand (and here, the feet)</term> is the art by which the hands and feet are understood for making judgments, along with all the rest.

**Physiognomy** <term>the practice of assessing a person's character or fortune from their outer appearance, especially the face</term> is the art by which judgment is made from the faces of men, and from all that which is above the shoulders.

**Constitution** or **proportion** <note>original: "Habitus aut proportio"</note> is the art by which the whole human body and its stature is measured, according to the science of Geometry, etc.

**Geomancy** <term>divination by interpreting markings on the ground or patterns formed by earth</term> is the art of points lacking a natural foundation; its power is situated in the stars, whether it achieves any effect or not.

**Pyromancy** <term>divination by means of fire</term> gives signs in fire alone and lacks a foundation; therefore its demonstrations are uncertain.

**Hydromancy** <term>divination by means of water</term> is the art which is performed through water, containing no more of the actual art than what the practitioner himself creates.

**Austromancy** <term>divination by observing the winds, their direction, and their sounds</term> is the art of the winds which is performed...

->e 4<- <note>This is a signature mark, used by early printers to keep the pages of a book in the correct order for binding.</note>

->**THEOPHR. PARAC.**<- <note>Abbreviation for Theophrastus Paracelsus, the author's name.</note>

...according to the courses of those [winds] and it happens not as any specific art.

Now you have the divisions and definitions of the individual types of **Astronomy**, so that this art can be understood better and with a greater foundation. There are twenty **members** <note>original: "Membrorum"; referring to the twenty specific branches of occult science listed in the preceding pages</note> which we have set forth, by which all powers of understanding, as well as operations, are included—indeed, everything the stars are capable of. In fact, these are the media through which the **stars** <note>original: "fydera"</note> operate and achieve their effect.

Previously I prescribed eight stars, or an eight-fold nature, which you shall understand in this way: Like always seeks its medium in what is like itself, and nothing is perfected outside of its like. For example:

Integrity is fulfilled through the upright, and every theft by a thief; for no upright man steals, nor can any thief be upright; therefore everything remains and operates in its own likeness. For this reason, there is a **Star** <note>original: "Astrum"</note> in the **firmament** <term>the sphere of the heavens or the sky</term> which has dominion over all things, in which all the **secrets** <note>original: "arcana"</note> of nature are contained; and it is the primary force that operates there, ruling and governing all others. In that star exists all **animal wisdom** <note>original: "brutalis fapientia"; referring to the instinctive intelligence of the natural world rather than human reason</note>, just as we receive flesh and blood from the earth: also **carnal wisdom**, reason, and...

<meta>catchword: art</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus concludes his definition of the twenty branches of "Astronomy" and introduces the core occult principle that "like attracts like," arguing that celestial bodies influence the world through specific mediums that match their own nature.</summary>

<keywords>Paracelsus, Astronomy, Firmament, Arcana, Animal Wisdom, Likeness, Stars, Occult Philosophy</keywords>

->**ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCE**<-

<margin>e 5</margin>

...arts we have from this star: for these **stars** <note>original: "astra"; here referring to spiritual or celestial influences</note> give and distribute to the mortal body, and they operate according to what each individual receives and conceives. There are another four stars in the **four elements** <term>the basic building blocks of the universe: earth, water, air, and fire</term>: for the earth also has its own star, its own course, progress, and order, just like the **firmament** <term>the vault of the heavens</term>, except that these are specifically related back to the celestial firmament. Likewise, a firmament is contained in water just as it is in the earth, and also in fire and in air. 

For this reason, the **celestial star** <note>original: "supernum astrum"</note> possesses these other stars of the elements as its **mediums** <note>the channels or tools through which a force acts</note>, and through these mediums it fulfills its **predestined operations** <note>the natural effects or "fates" assigned to the stars by God</note>. For within them is an inclination that cannot be twisted or turned away; and through such operations of the celestial and inferior stars, all things bear fruit, become fertile, and reach their destined end.

Therefore, there are **eight stars**: the celestial one, and the four inferior ones, along with three others. The celestial star is the active force, while the four inferior ones serve as the **medium**; in this way, **like operates upon its like**. There is yet another star, and also one medium similar to the celestial, composed of the superior star... <note>The text cuts off here, continuing the thought on the next page.</note>

<summary>Paracelsus explains that the celestial stars do not act directly on the world but through "inferior stars" located within the four elements. He introduces a system of eight primary influences, emphasizing that celestial forces require a corresponding "medium" on earth to achieve their natural purpose.</summary>

->### THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS 68<-

...and elements, which is what man is, whom the **stars** <note>original: "astra"</note> have begotten: for the elements provide one part in man, such as the **brute body** <note>the physical, animalistic body that lacks a soul</note>; the other part, namely **brute wisdom** <note>natural instinct or worldly intelligence</note>, the **celestial star** <note>original: "supernum astrum"</note> gives.

Man is also subject to four others, for these must likewise rule him. And thus the **four complexions** <term>the four temperaments or humors that were thought to determine a person's physical and mental health</term> arise from the stars, such as **melancholy**, **phlegm**, **blood**, and **choler** <note>yellow bile</note>, not [merely] in the body but as in the **essence of Mars** <note>referring to the planetary influence of Mars on the human constitution</note>. Man, therefore, is subject to five stars and is their son; let these words suffice concerning these matters for now.

# ON THE COMPOSITION
## *of the human race.*

**S**ince **Astronomy** <term>the study of the stars, which in Paracelsus's time included what we now call astrology</term> was created, given, and discovered entirely for the sake of man, and since one ought necessarily to recognize here that it concerns him alone, how Christian it is for a man to know himself well and rightly, and to know what manner of being exists created within him; for from that knowledge [he gains] the greatest...

<meta>The text ends mid-sentence; the catchword "mam" indicates the next page begins with the completion of the word "maximam," likely meaning "the greatest wisdom" or "the greatest benefit."</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus argues that the human "complexions" (the four humors) are not just physical traits but are derived from celestial essences. He begins a new section asserting that astronomy is a vital tool for Christian self-knowledge because man is the ultimate purpose of the stars.</summary>

<meta>This page continues from page 68, completing the sentence beginning with "maximam" (the greatest). It continues Paracelsus's argument that man is the culmination of creation and that studying astronomy is the key to understanding man's "astral body."</meta>

->### THE SCIENCE OF ASTRONOMY 69<-

...benefit he will obtain, especially since he will have known who and what he himself is, so that in him eternal wisdom might rule all these things; he himself also ought to choose and hold fast [to what is good], and know what in each thing to reject. First, that which pertains to the **astral body** <term>in Paracelsian thought, a subtle "star-body" that connects the physical body to the heavens and governs instincts and movements</term> must be known and discovered from Astronomy, so it may be known from whence the **first man** <note>Adam</note> is and comes, from whom we all have been begotten and born. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;He was a work [of God], and we also are the work of our parents, counting in reverse order back to the first man, who was not the work of men, but of God. Wherefore Luke the Evangelist wrote of **Christ**, that he was supposed to be the son of Joseph: this is understood more as a "work" <note>referring to the biological lineage or the legal status of Joseph as a father, rather than the divine origin</note>: for any son is the work of his father **Heli** <note>referring to the genealogy in the Gospel of Luke 3:23, where Heli is listed as the father of Joseph</note>, etc. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;You should understand a "finished work" in this way: man was made by the hand of God alone, for he was indeed made last among the first [creations]: God formed him in his own image, and no one else besides God [formed him], and he was formed last as the first <note>meaning man was the final, crowning achievement of the creation week</note>. Yet He made him from a **mass, from mud, and from dust** <note>original: "massa, limo, pulvere"</note>, which is to be understood thus: First God created all things, then from all things He made man. For example: first all the colors are made, then the painter [applies them]...

<meta>The text ends mid-sentence; the catchword "hac" indicates the next page continues the analogy of the painter and his colors.</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus explains that through astronomy, one learns about the "astral body" and man's divine origin. He posits that man is the final "work" of God, created from the essence of all preceding creations, much like a painter uses previously prepared colors to create a final image.</summary>

<keywords>Astral Body, Adam, Image of God, Genealogy of Christ, Heli, Microcosm, Creation, Elements, Alchemy of Creation</keywords>

--- Page 96 ---
<meta>This page continues from page 69, completing the analogy of the painter. It develops the argument that man is a "microcosm" (a small world) containing the essence of the entire "macrocosm" (the universe).</meta>

->### THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS 70<-

...in the same way that colors make the painter <note>i.e., provide him with the means to perform his work</note>. Another example: out of many things—perhaps sixty or seventy ingredients—a physician composes a medicine which cannot be made unless all these are joined together. Since, therefore, man makes nothing except as a whole and complete work, having all the instruments necessary for this purpose, we ought to recognize through this evidence that God, for His own work—namely man—took up the **greatest composite** <note>original: "maximum compositum," referring to man as the ultimate mixture of all universal ingredients</note>. This was so that He might create man from all His creatures gathered together into one **mass**, and from that mass He made man.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In other respects, God created all things through His word, and all creatures were made from nothing—except for man. Man He made from one body, which was called a **mass** or **dust**. Just as Christ also healed the sick by His word alone, yet among others, He restored sight to the blind man using spit <note>referring to the Gospel of John 9:6, where Jesus heals a man born blind using clay made from saliva</note>—which He could have done by word alone without the spit, but it seemed right to Him to do it that way—this same principle must be understood regarding the "mass." 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;When, therefore, man was made by God, He composed him from all the **simples** <term>in early medicine and alchemy, "simples" are the basic, unmixed substances or medicinal plants that form the building blocks of more complex remedies</term> of heaven and earth which God Himself created. From all of them He extracted the **fifth being** <term>original: "quintum esse," or the quintessence; in Paracelsian thought, this is the purest, most potent essence of a substance, the spiritual core that remains after the four physical elements are removed</term>; that is, He reduced the "fifth being" into a single mass, which 

<meta>The text ends mid-sentence; the catchword "massa" indicates the next page will continue describing the nature of this primordial substance from which man was formed.</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus explains that man is a "maximum compositum," unique because he was crafted from the essence (quintessence) of all other created things rather than from nothing. He uses analogies of a physician's medicine and Christ's use of physical means to heal to explain why God used a "mass" of materials to create humanity.</summary>

->## ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCE<-

The mass is called the **lesser world**, because the entire universe was enclosed from the **great world** <note>original: "magno mundo," the macrocosm or outer universe</note> into a small work; and man is the lesser world, while the world in which man dwells is the greater world. There is no other greater essence upon the earth. For this reason, man is called the **fifth essence** <note>original: "essentia quinta"</note>, especially because he was made from the four elements and from one **star** <note>original: "astro"; in Paracelsian thought, this refers to the "firmament" or sidereal body within man</note>, and he has retained those five essences. 

Although much has been said elsewhere regarding the **fifth essence** <term>original: "quinta essentia"; the purest, most concentrated essence of a substance</term>, nonetheless it is not properly called anything other than the extraction of the pure from the impure. It ought not to be called the fifth essence, but a **secret** <term>original: "arcanum"; a hidden, powerful spiritual or medicinal force</term>; for in this extraction nothing remains <note>referring to the waste of the process</note>, but in the mass you remain, with the four essences unharmed, which the mass contains completely within itself. 

Therefore, this work of God was encompassed and completed in **Adam**, so that the **machine of the world** <note>original: "machina mundi"; a common Renaissance term describing the universe as a complex, divinely-ordered system</note> was made into man, transmuted into flesh and blood. Thus, the great world was made man, and his flesh and blood became the **microcosm** <term>original: "microcosmus"; the "small world," the concept that the human body is a miniature map of the entire universe</term>. From this it follows that the entire machine of the world surrounds man, and that he exists in the middle of it as the center of all creatures. Wherefore, it must be observed with the greatest diligence that since man exists as the center, all cir... <note>The text cuts off mid-word, likely continuing to discuss the "circles" of the heavens or the surrounding environment.</note>

<summary>Paracelsus explains that man is a "microcosm" created from the "machine of the world," containing the essences of the four elements and the stars. He defines man as the "center of all creatures," physically embodying the same substances as the entire universe.</summary>

### PARTICULAR PROOF
### regarding Astrological science.

After having spoken about the **generation** <note>The process of being born or coming into being.</note> of man, it is necessary to write about his **sustenance** <note>How a person is maintained and kept alive through cosmic forces.</note>; once this is known, **Astrology** is proven. Understand the matter in this way: You see in man an innate and inborn power which draws the external spheres to himself and into himself. He is even sustained by these attracted forces, becoming healthy or infirm according to that which he has received into himself.

Perceive the matter instead by this example: there are two drawing forces. One belongs to the **elemental body**, and the other belongs to the **sidereal body** <term>original: "syderici"; the "astral" or star-body that mirrors the heavens</term>. These forces manifest as **hunger** and **thirst** in this manner: the rays of the external spheres are extended even unto us...

<summary>Paracelsus argues that the Astronomer must view man as a microcosm—the center of the universe's influence. He introduces the idea that human health and survival depend on "drawing in" celestial forces, comparing this cosmic attraction to the physical sensations of hunger and thirst.</summary>

# 74
## THEOPHRATUS PARACELSUS

...that these rays should come into us must happen through us, just as the spheres of fruit trees eat and produce their fruits to reach us, from the middle of their roots to the **shoots** <note>original: "locustas"; in this botanical context, it refers to the tender branches or shoots of a plant.</note>. Now, that attractive force and the hungry and thirsty nature which is within us compels us to feed on such fruits: the rays of these fruits have already entered into us and remain.

The same must be understood regarding bread: the sphere of the earth brings this forth for us, and through our power of attraction, we receive it into our body. Likewise, we also draw drink from the shoots of its own sphere into ourselves. By this kind of order of **predestination** <note>In Paracelsus’s view, this is not just fate, but the pre-arranged natural design of how life is sustained.</note> man is led, sustained, and lives naturally. 

Just as you have now understood that the body uses external rays through an attractive force—namely, hunger and thirst—likewise you ought to recognize and know that the **sidereal body** <term>original: "fydereum" (sidereum); the astral or star-body that connects the human spirit to the heavens</term> of man observes the same order according to its nature. It receives what is necessary for itself from the external spheres, even through the hunger and thirst of that same body—that is, the sidereal body. It was created and made in this order; and just as bodily food and drink are ordained for man from all the elements, he finds in them everything by which the human body...

...[the human body] needs, it must receive from them: similarly, God gave to the stars and constellations all **animal wisdom** <note>original: "brutinam sapientiã"; this refers to the instinctive, natural intelligence shared by animals and the physical side of humanity.</note>, understanding, aptitude, art, and whatever man can and should know in a worldly and transitory way. For just as those things grow, so too do <term>Music</term> <note>In the Renaissance, music was studied as a mathematical and celestial science tied to the harmony of the spheres.</note>, the art of metals, <term>Medicine</term>, and other such "plantations" grow. Whatever the earthly and elemental body needs, it finds this very thing in the wisdom of the constellations and stars. Indeed, all the wisdom that man possesses—whether it be good or evil—he must learn from the stars, with the exception of two: namely, Justice and the Holy Scripture. These proceed without any intermediary <note>original: "sine medio"; meaning they are granted directly by God rather than through the natural influence of the stars.</note> from the **Holy Spirit**. 

Therefore, since the <term>Light of Nature</term> <note>original: "lux naturalis"; a central concept in Paracelsian philosophy referring to the innate intelligence and guidance provided by the natural world.</note> is situated entirely within the stars and placed there by God, and since man must receive this from them just as he receives food from the earth in which he was born, he is likewise also born from the stars, etc. However, there are two wisdoms in which we are born: the first being Christian wisdom, as Christ speaks:

> I was born for this, [and for this I came into the world...]

->THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS<-

Christ was born in eternal wisdom. From this it follows that, since we are not born in transitory wisdom <note>original: "transitoria sapientia"; this refers to the temporary, worldly knowledge associated with the stars and the physical world.</note>, we ought not to use it for any purpose other than for the necessity of the physical body. However, we ought to use that wisdom in which Christ was born, and learn it for this end, just as Christ says:

> *Let him who thirsts come to me,* <note>original: "Qui sitit veniat ad me"; a reference to John 7:37.</note>

This means that through hunger and thirst you shall receive nourishment from the earth; and through hunger and thirst it is necessary for you to receive **animal wisdom** <note>original: "brutinam sapientiam"; the instinctive, natural intelligence that Paracelsus believes humans share with animals, governed by the stars.</note>; and thirdly, through hunger and thirst it is proper for you to receive eternal wisdom. For in hunger and thirst there exists an attracting force by which you must be fed. For this reason, we hunger and thirst in three ways.

I have not proposed to write in this place about those things which attract eternal wisdom, but only to declare those things which attract animal wisdom, so that it may be known from where we ought to derive our animal reason, what kind of reason exists within us, and so that this may be distinguished from the eternal reason—for **sidereal wisdom** <note>original: "syderea sapientia"; wisdom originating from the influence of the stars.</note> is foolishness before God. For this cause a wise man has said:

> *The wise man shall rule over the stars,* <note>original: "Sapiens dominabitur astris"; a famous Renaissance maxim suggesting that while the stars influence the physical and "animal" mind, the enlightened human soul can transcend that influence.</note>

as if he were saying: eternal wisdom has dominion over animal wisdom. It must be understood more broadly that nature...

...[natu]ral wisdom was given to the body and not to the soul; wherefore those things which concern the soul cannot be contaminated by the **light of nature** <note>original: "luce naturae"; in Paracelsian thought, this refers to the innate intelligence of the natural world and the stars, which teaches humans medicine, craft, and philosophy.</note>, but this light ought to be applied only to nature. Mortals have not yet discovered these secrets and similar things; yet if they drank from the true source, these mysteries would by no means be hidden from them.

There are also stone-cutters in the mansions of the planets, from whom all stone-cutters learn their art; and if our craftsmen knew those celestial stone-cutters, they would shape their stone images as if they were made of clay; nevertheless, the results would be not a hair's breadth worse or softer <note>original: "pilo deteriores aut molliores"; meaning the stones would remain hard and durable despite being worked with the ease of soft materials.</note> [than if carved traditionally]. ->ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCE<-

f 3

...Lucifer and his colleagues alienated and changed themselves from their original creation, so too this can happen within the stars: for which reason, equally opposing conceptions can be born, acting as corrupters and enemies of all true arts—of medicine, and of all other parts of natural wisdom <term>sapientia naturalis: the inherent wisdom found within the natural world, which man must learn to decipher.</term>. Therefore, one should not believe every spirit, since they exist in two conditions, just as in heaven two types of angels dwell: one part remains in that same state in which they were created by God, but the other part makes itself into something else. The Star acts in this way, and so does man, who is the "son" of the Star, when he is unwilling to rule himself according to the judgment that is innate to him from the **light of nature**.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;For this reason, **Astronomy** is highly necessary, and men who desire to be [wise] ought to know it perfectly; for it teaches how to discern those two spirits in the **light of nature**, just as one distinguishes true light from false, and just as **Holy Scripture** teaches us to know God and separate Him from the devil, and to discern true prophets from false ones, and so forth. I can say in truth that Astronomy contains the most massive secrets <term>arcana: profound, hidden mysteries or the inner spiritual "virtues" of natural things that are not visible to the naked eye.</term>, and furthermore, that without it, nothing can be learned by anyone. This is the very thing I wish to demonstrate to you. We have said before that through...

<summary>Paracelsus explains that both the stars and humanity can deviate from their divine purpose, much like the fallen angels. He argues that the study of Astronomy is vital because it provides the discernment necessary to distinguish true natural wisdom from corrupting influences.</summary>

<keywords>Astronomy, Lucifer, fallen angels, Light of Nature, arcana, natural wisdom, discernment, corruption of arts</keywords>

...perpetual wisdom would be one thing, and the natural another. The eternal remains in that same state, constant and immovable; but the natural produces a false spirit from its own corrupted conception. Now know that this spirit exalts itself, so that it desires to rule over eternal wisdom, just as Lucifer himself did. From this follows a distortion of **Holy Scripture**, so that each person, according to their own birth, twists it like a "nose of wax" <note>original: "cæreum nasum". A common 16th-century metaphor for a text that is easily manipulated or reshaped to suit a person's personal whims or biases.</note>, yet no one does so in the same way as another. 

Since, therefore, **Astronomy** is able to perceive this through the hour of birth <term>hour of birth: In Paracelsian thought, the celestial alignment at the moment of birth determines a person's "inner stars" or natural character and temperament.</term>, and to detect that spirit as it truly is—by which "father" and "mother" it was begotten and produced, and what kind of "son" it exists as—nothing remains so hidden within a person that it is not made manifest through the Astronomical art. Who, then, would not exalt and praise the knowledge of this science? For through it, those people are recognized who fill the ranks of false prophets and from whom such prophets arise; it would have been better for them if they had not been born in such a likeness. Through **Astronomy**, also, one recognizes error-prone workers of the mechanical arts <term>mechanical arts: practical trades and crafts involving physical labor and materials, such as masonry, weaving, or metalworking.</term> and wandering physicians.

Understand the matter in this way: as has been said thus far, what sort of man is created and born, and who hungers and thirsts, the elementary star... <meta>catchword: dere- (likely "syderis" or "syderum", referring to the stars)</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus describes how natural wisdom can be corrupted by pride, leading individuals to manipulate Scripture. He argues that Astronomy is a divine tool for unmasking these "false spirits" and identifying incompetence in both spiritual leaders and practical craftsmen.</summary>

-># ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCE<-

...and the **sidereal body** <note>original: *sidereum corpus*; in Paracelsian thought, this is the "astral" or "star-led" body that mediates between the physical flesh and the immortal soul.</note> in its desire and according to that which conception gave it, must be fed and watered; likewise, whatever at that moment was instilled by the **influences** <note>astrological emanations or "outflowings" from the planets and stars believed to shape a person's character.</note> clings to it. From there it follows that the Astrologer can prescribe those same powers, nature, properties, essence, the entire harmony, and the constellations: for in this way have been discovered and proven the wonderful births of both good and perverse children. 

But truly, that which is governed by eternal wisdom is for God, not for men, to know, because the angelic is not human. However, that which lives according to nature—as the majority of men do—can contain nothing so hidden within itself that it is not known to Astrology. Therefore, in the generation of men, a **figure of the heavens** <term>figure of the heavens: A natal chart or horoscope depicting the positions of the stars and planets at the moment of birth.</term> can be erected, by which the properties of the stars can be known and revealed, as well as their particular manner. 

By this reasoning, Astronomy understands whatever nature, wisdom, and art of the stars those who fulfill their operations may create in the **brute man** <term>brute man: The animalistic or purely physical aspect of a human being. Paracelsus argued that while the soul is free, the "brute" part of man is subject to the laws of nature and the stars.</term>. Wherefore the Astrologer can judge man as a brute, since such a conjunction and genuine harmony exists within him. Truly, if, as would be right, the Astrologer...

->### THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS<-

...[Astro]logy <note>Completing the word "Astrologia" from the previous page.</note> were treated from a proper foundation, and the **birth charts** <term>nativities: Astrological charts calculated for the moment of a person's birth to determine their character and destiny.</term> of men were examined with sound judgment—so that those born under them might be guided according to the nature of those **influences** <note>original: *influentiarum*; the celestial "flow" of energy from the stars into the physical world.</note>—many evils could be avoided. For the "center," as an inclination, must proceed from one center to another <note>This refers to the Paracelsian concept that the "center" of a star or planet communicates directly with the corresponding "center" or organ within a human being.</note>.

## PROOF OF THE SCIENCE OF Magic

First, one must understand what **Magic** <note>To Paracelsus, Magic is not "sorcery" but a "true science" that works with the hidden, natural powers of the universe.</note> is. Therefore, Magic is the true science by which **celestial power** <note>original: *virtus coelestis*; the active, spiritual energy or "virtue" of the heavens.</note> is drawn into a medium, and through that, its works are accomplished. The medium is the "center," and that center is **man**. Thus, through man, celestial power can be imprinted upon man, since there is found within man the same capacity for action that exists within a constellation.

Furthermore, there is a certain secret operation contained within Magic that it performs itself—namely, when it uses this art of its own accord. This happens when the nature of the stars or planets performs the very thing the **Magician** <term>Magus: A practitioner of natural magic who understands how to bridge the gap between the stars and the earthly realm.</term> ought to have done. Although the Magician himself is the medium and the center, and can join the workings of the stars with humanity so that they may be completed through men:

<meta>catchword: is</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus argues that Astrology can be used to avoid earthly misfortunes and then defines "Magic" as a natural science. He explains that man acts as a "medium" or "center" through which the powers of the stars are funneled into the physical world.</summary>

...there is still something granted to the art, so that from the powers of the art another **medium** <term>medium: An intermediary or channel through which spiritual or celestial forces are manifested in the physical world.</term> (by which every subject is understood as one) [is used], through which the same operation is completed just as well as through man, who is the true medium. Therefore, there are three types of operations in the **Magic art** <note>original: *arte Magica*; here referring to the "true science" of natural forces.</note>, one of which is fulfilled by **nature** itself and through itself—whether it chooses and sets before itself a man through whom it works, bestowing upon him its influences, good or bad; or whether it imprints itself upon some other subject through which it might work, such as in **images** <note>original: *imagines*; likely referring to astrological talismans or figures inscribed with celestial symbols.</note>, **stones**, **herbs**, or **words**. Or it may produce a **Comet**, **likenesses**, **halos** <note>original: *halones*; atmospheric phenomena like rings around the sun or moon, interpreted as signs.</note>, and other non-natural generations of the stars.

Therefore, nature can prepare its magic powers by itself, and through them also complete operations—as if a miracle were occurring in the eyes of the **ignorant common folk** <note>original: *vulgus ignarum*; Paracelsus often contrasts the true philosopher with the unlearned public who mistake natural magic for supernatural miracles.</note>, and such signs might rain down or fall into a sanctuary, which nature nevertheless performed in no other way than magically. Many things are done and similar things happen in this way, which are not understood as they truly are by the ignorant.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;So that you may understand even better whatever nature can do and complete in a **foreign body** <note>original: *corpus extraneum*; a body or object outside of the "natural" expected order.</note>, man himself is also able to bring similar operations to that point, so that...

<summary>Paracelsus explains that nature can perform "magical" operations through various mediums other than man, such as herbs, stones, and atmospheric signs. He emphasizes that what the public calls "miracles" are actually natural processes of this magic science.</summary>

...a certain drunken sleepiness. They say that <term>Magic</term> <note>original: *Magia*; here referring to the "Great Art" of directing natural and celestial forces.</note> is an art and science to be admired for its operations. Yet, since we know what Christ spoke:

> If you have constant faith, you shall do greater things than these, etc. <note>original: *Si fidem constantē habueritis, his maiora facietis*. A reference to the Gospel of John 14:12, where Jesus promises his followers will perform works even greater than his own.</note>

And since, while performing miracles, he asserted that the disciples would do greater things than he: if nature is capable of anything by its own art, why should we not also be able to perform more in it than nature itself can? Nature was clearly created for our sake; for this reason, we have power and dominion over it. A wise man rules nature; nature does not rule the wise man. And if Sacred Scripture can do more through faith than Christ did in charity toward his neighbor, why shall we also not be able to do more than the <term>stars</term> <note>original: *astra*; the celestial bodies which Paracelsus believed exerted a physical and spiritual influence on humanity.</note>, since Christ speaks of us and not of the constellations, and those things are subjected to us along with everything else?
