<page-num>88</page-num>

->THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS<-

that is, in that very place heat also exists, for the light of day is situated within heat. How can it be that this light of day, with its innate heat, might be drawn by a human from the heavens down into some object, so that this light overcomes our eyes and their own light, and its heat cannot be endured by touch? This is clearly demonstrated by experiment through the roundness of a crystalline sphere, which is later called a **beryl** <note>original: *berillus*; here referring to a "burning glass" or magnifying lens used to focus sunlight to start fires.</note>. By its brilliance, this lens dulls and darkens any eyes, and it ignites and burns all things; the Sun itself can accomplish no more than this.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Since, therefore, the **Magician** <term>Magus</term> <note>original: *Magus*; a practitioner who works with the hidden forces of nature and the stars.</note> is able to likewise draw any powers of the stars into an object prepared by him, could he not similarly fashion **images** <note>original: *imagines*; these are astrological talismans or figures inscribed with celestial symbols to capture specific planetary influences.</note> for recovering and preserving health, as well as for driving away illness? Could he not move or **mortify** <note>original: *mortificare*; in a medical sense, to deaden or cause tissue to wither through external influence.</note> **natural flesh** <note>original: *naturalem carnem*; corrected from the printer's error "natnralem".</note> toward this or that end? Poison can arise from the earth, and likewise something similar can arise from the heavens, whether in images, herbs, stones, or words. All of this is situated in this fact: namely, insofar as the **star** <term>astrum</term> <note>original: *astrum*; the celestial body or the spiritual influence it radiates.</note> carries its own impregnation, and furthermore as these conceptions have been directed. It is not that man alone does this, but nature also above him...

<meta>catchword: herself (ipsam)</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus uses the analogy of a burning glass (beryl) to show how celestial heat and light can be concentrated into a physical object. He argues that just as a lens focuses the Sun, a Magician can focus the virtues of the stars into talismans (images) for healing or causing change in the physical body.</summary>

<keywords>burning glass, beryl, Magus, astrological images, talismans, celestial poison, Light of Nature, astrum, healing virtues</keywords>

--- Page 121 ---
<meta>Continuity: This page concludes the argument that human arts—especially Magic and Medicine—are imitations of Nature's own internal workings. It moves from the specific example of the burning lens on the previous page to the general principle that a true Magician is a student of Nature, culminating in the biblical example of the Three Magi.</meta>

<page-num>89</page-num>

### ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCE 89

<note>The text on the left margin is partially obscured by the book's binding gutter.</note>

Nature herself executes this in manifold ways. If these things are possible for nature, why should they not be possible for men? For nature provides an example to humans so that they might imitate her in their own operations. To her belongs the **medical art** <note>original: *ars medica*; Paracelsus believed true medicine was found in nature, not just in books.</note>, any of the **mechanical arts** <note>original: *mechanicę*; the practical or applied sciences.</note>, and all other arts are contained within her, as are all **animal instincts** <note>original: *brutales promptitudines*; literally "brute readiness" or the natural, unthinking skills of animals.</note>, and so on. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;From this it follows, as stated more often above, that while one man learns from another, he who is taught by nature excels all others in his art. Such a man is born like a **Comet** <term>Cometa</term> <note>original: *Cometa*; in Paracelsian thought, a comet represents a rare, extraordinary person or event that breaks the standard rules of the stars.</note>, and is therefore different from one born under a different star. It is not otherwise possible for a **Magician** <term>Magus</term> to produce a similarly learned and industrious man through the science of Magic except in the likeness of a Comet. These are the mysteries and great works of God, whose praise the heavens thus declare, and the **firmament** <note>original: *firmamentum*; the vault of the heavens where the stars are fixed.</note> declares His work through the Magicians. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;We also find in the **Gospel** <note>original: *Euangelio*</note> how wonderfully, through the **Magi** from **Sheba** and **Tarshish** <note>original: *Saba Tharſis*; Paracelsus identifies the "Wise Men" from the East with these biblical regions.</note>, the rising of Christ in Bethlehem was made known, and what the higher heaven and firmament announced from the **secrets** <note>original: *arcanis*; the hidden, spiritual core of natural things.</note> of God. From these things which have been said, Magic is sufficiently explained and proven. 

->Whatever else is necessary to know<-
->more broadly concerning this,<-
->I shall treat in its<-
->own practice.<-

<summary>Paracelsus argues that all human arts are imitations of Nature and that the greatest practitioners are those taught directly by Nature, whom he compares to rare comets. He cites the biblical Magi as the ultimate example of those who read God's secrets through the stars.</summary>

<keywords>Nature, medical art, mechanical arts, Comet, Magus, Firmament, Gospel, Magi, Sheba, Tarshish, Arcanum, astronomical science</keywords>

<header>THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS</header>

### PROOF FOR THE SCIENCE
->of divination.<-

**I**t often happens that a certain **Astronomy** <term>Astronomia</term> <note>In the Paracelsian view, Astronomy is not merely the mathematical study of stars, but the lived relationship between the "inner stars" of a human being and the outer heavens.</note> produces itself out of itself, and even completes astronomical operations without [human] diligence or art. Although this does not occur in everyone, it happens in those who are of an industrious and good condition, such as those who are sober and well-behaved and do not impede nature. The ancients were very careful not to contaminate their body and soul, so that such operations might be fulfilled in them. Indeed, this Astronomy, born of itself, directs itself toward heavenly operations, and this species of Astronomy is called **Divination** <term>Divinatio</term>. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;And since this **firmamental** <note>original: *firmamentalis*; pertaining to the firmament or the sphere of the fixed stars.</note> operation occurs apart from [technical] knowledge of Astronomy, diviners were once held in the highest esteem by the common people as **Gods** <note>original: *Diis*</note>. Understand that these operations are manifested through **dreams** <note>original: *somnia*</note>, through the soul and its contemplations, and also through animals. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Divinations were once held in the highest esteem; later, however, when some **correspondence** <note>original: *concordantia*; a meaningful coincidence or harmony between an omen and a later event.</note> occurred to which heavenly operations sometimes conformed and were fulfilled—yet because they did not understand [the cause], they placed no faith in it.

<summary>Paracelsus explains that "Divination" is actually a natural, internal form of Astronomy that functions without formal study in people who keep their minds and bodies pure. He notes that while the ancients deified those with this gift, modern people often dismiss it because they do not understand the underlying celestial harmony.</summary>

<keywords>Divination, Astronomy, firmamental operations, dreams, ancients, correspondence, soul, animal omens, Paracelsus</keywords>

--- Page 123 ---
<meta>Continuity: This page continues Paracelsus’s argument that divination is a natural function of the "sidereal body" rather than a supernatural miracle. He explains that what the common people mistake for angelic intervention is actually the inner astral self communicating with the outer stars during sleep.</meta>

<page-num>91</page-num>
<header>ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCE</header>

...and led astray by both mistrust and incredulity, they have erred greatly. For when faith is placed in men of this kind, and a **deceitful star** <note>original: *astrum fraudulentum*; Paracelsus suggests that not all celestial influences are reliable or clear, sometimes leading to false omens.</note> misleads them, many opinions regarding presages occur which would not be accepted otherwise. Even though a species of **Astronomy** exists here, it is not a formal **art** <note>original: *ars*; referring to a technical or academic discipline that must be learned.</note>, but rather an operation born of its own nature.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;There is a common proverb: "My **angel**," they say, "revealed this to me." Thus, a natural operation of this kind is called an "angel" by the ignorant; and as if it were performed by God, it is attributed to an angel, as though there were a medium between God and the event—whereas it is actually only a celestial operation. The origin of **divinations** is that man possesses a **sidereal body** <term>sidereal body</term> <note>original: *syderum corpus*; in Paracelsian thought, this is an invisible "astral body" that connects the human spirit to the firmament, allowing for the reception of celestial influence.</note> within himself. This is united with the external stars, and both "talk" together when that sidereal body is free from the [heavy] elements and unhindered, as occurs in sleep. It is then in its state of operation, and its effects follow after dreams. At that time, just as the star operates, so too does the event occur; and according to the good or bad disposition or preparation of the constellations or stars, a dream is instilled and prefigured. It happens quite often that through a suitable star of this kind, and through a well-disposed sidereal body, whatever future things...

<summary>Paracelsus argues that divination is not a miracle performed by angels, but a natural interaction between a person's "sidereal body" and the heavens. He explains that during sleep, this inner astral self communicates with the stars, allowing future events to be prefigured in dreams.</summary>

...were predicted, and indeed they came to pass. For this reason, many **medicines** have been discovered, by which numerous illnesses were cured and the sick set free from them. Many **treasures** and similar hidden things were found, so that for this reason it could be compared to a great **art**: for the **star** <note>original: *sydus*; in this context, it refers to the collective influence of the heavens or a specific celestial body.</note> reveals everything that is to come; nothing is hidden from it, whether in the past or the present. Therefore, when in a similar way a well-disposed **sidereal body** is found by a prepared star, wonders are revealed to it—things that are to come and things that are present. 

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In this way, women and elderly people—though entirely unskilled—have often **prophesied**, in a kind of **fury** <note>original: *furore*; likely referring to a trance or a state of being "seized" by natural inspiration, rather than anger.</note>, things which were later found and proven to be true over a long period of time in a marvelous way. There were also many learned men who became so through their sidereal body, especially where they exercised themselves in the **influence** <term>influence</term> <note>original: *influentia*; literally a "flowing in" of celestial power or qualities from the stars into the human person.</note> in which they were born, and subsequently drew the inclination of their own star to themselves—just as one draws in the rays of the Sun (and as the example teaches) for nourishment. And so, in similar things through **contemplations**, they discovered and acquired admirable **wisdom**: yet they did so purely <note>original: *brutaliter*; here meaning "naturally" or "of the animal/astral soul," distinguishing it from spiritual revelation that comes directly from God.</note>, not from on high, but from the stars alone. To those also at that time—

<header>ASTRONOMICAL KNOWLEDGE 93</header>

In the times when the **heaven** <term>heaven</term> <note>original: *coelum*; in the Paracelsian worldview, the "heaven" is the source of the "Light of Nature," the celestial influence that instructs the human mind and provides natural wisdom.</note> existed in such a disposition and generated within itself the **sidereal body**, <term>sidereal body</term> <note>original: *corpus sydereum*; the "astral" or star-born part of a human being that acts as an intermediary between the physical flesh and the immortal spirit.</note> it produced wonderful human talents: writers, teachers, and interpreters of **Holy Scripture**, philosophers and the like, each according to his own **constellation**. Nevertheless, their writings and doctrine ought not to be considered holy, even though they may have the finest appearance; for the **star** and its **influence** <term>influence</term> <note>original: *influentia*; literally a "flowing in" of celestial power or qualities from the stars into the terrestrial world.</note> produced them as they are. This is the work of the **spirit of nature**, not of God, who performed such things.

Likewise, it has very often happened that such an operation issued from a man’s mind in a wondrous way, changing the man entirely in his mind and heart so that he appeared holy; yet, it was actually nothing more than a **drunken star**. <note>original: *ebrium sydus*; a metaphor for a celestial influence that intoxicates the mind, creating a natural state of ecstasy or brilliance that mimics divine grace but remains purely "astral."</note> Just as wine changes a man, he too was changed. For this reason, it is especially necessary to understand **Astronomy** <note>To Paracelsus, "Astronomy" was the study of the inner stars of man and how they resonate with the outer stars of the firmament.</note> perfectly; for such intoxicated teachers and writers lead many astray. They err in the **Spirit of God** as well as in the **Light of Nature**; <term>Light of Nature</term> <note>original: *lux naturae*; the innate wisdom or "instruction" provided to humans by the stars, distinct from the spiritual revelation of God.</note> they waver and vary like **dreams**. For they bring forth many things which no man...

->**THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS**<-

can understand, so that they are even as nothing. The **stars** <term>stars</term> <note>original: *sydera*; in this context, Paracelsus refers to the celestial forces that govern the natural, "sidereal" part of every living creature.</note> exist with such great powers that they demonstrate their operations even in irrational creatures; for everything that lives has within itself a **sidereal spirit**, <term>sidereal spirit</term> <note>original: *spiritum sidereum*; the invisible, celestial breath or life-force that links a living being to the heavens.</note> which is manifested through its actions. As is the common opinion regarding the **peacock**, which, when it cries out at an unusual time, is said to foretell the death of its master. No man dies without it first being announced by some **omen**, <note>original: *praenuncio*; a fore-announcer or sign. Paracelsus believed that because man is a "microcosm" or small world, his death affects the "macrocosm" or large world.</note> and when someone dies, the **star** within him is deprived of its operations; this loss does not occur without a sign and a very great warning. 

An example is found in the **death of Christ**, <note>Paracelsus often uses Biblical events as physical, natural proofs of his theories. He argues that Christ’s death, as the death of the ultimate "Microcosm," caused a massive reaction in the "Macrocosm" of nature.</note> when the **veil of the temple was torn**, there was an **earthquake**, and an **eclipse** across the whole earth. <note>original: *velum templi scissum, terræmotus, & ecclipsis*.</note> Indeed, the stars and constellations are terrified by death, which is why no one dies without a foretoken: something is moved against nature, by which a man’s death can be known. For as often as the **dances of the dead** <note>original: *choreæ mortuorum*; likely a reference to the "Danse Macabre" or spectral visions of dancing skeletons, which were historically interpreted as omens of plague or mass death.</note> appear, a great mortality follows. 

More often in houses, the **noises of workers** <note>original: *laborantium strepitus*; Paracelsus is referring to "wraiths" or "fetches"—supernatural sounds of hammering or labor that seem to mimic the building of a coffin or the work the person did in life, preceding their death.</note> are heard, as if they were made by men; afterward, those who perform similar labors die. It must be known, therefore, that these signs are by no means **incantations**, <note>original: *incantations*; Paracelsus is distinguishing between "natural magic" (astronomy) and "sorcery" or "superstition." He argues these omens are biological and celestial laws, not witchcraft.</note> but natural operations which are thus completed in men. Mostly...

<summary>Paracelsus explains that all living things possess a sidereal spirit that reacts to death. He argues that omens—such as the peacock's cry, spectral noises, or the eclipse at Christ's crucifixion—are not miracles or sorcery, but natural celestial reactions to the cessation of life.</summary>

<header>ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCE 95</header>
<sig>g 3</sig>

Firstly, it must be noted that the stars are moved by the greatest compassion toward humans. For man was created in such a way that the whole of heaven is affected with pain along with him; and before death arrives, heaven sends forth forewarnings <note>original: *praesagia*; signs or omens of future events, often perceived in the natural world.</note> out of its great sorrow.

### PARTICULAR PROOF
->*into the science of Necromancy.*<- <note>original: *Nigromanticam*; in this context, it refers to a branch of natural science involving divination or the study of "shadows" and stellar images, rather than the modern sense of communicating with the literal dead.</note>

One of the branches of Astronomy is called **Necromancy**, so named because its practitioners used it more by night than by day; and those who first worked in it by night received those things which can also be accomplished by day. This art has been utterly rejected by those ignorant of it and by those who did not understand it; it has even been called and esteemed as diabolical, yet it is entirely natural and produced from the stars. Consider first the properties of **beryls**, <note>A transparent gemstone, often a variety of emerald or aquamarine, historically used as a "show-stone" for scrying or divination.</note> for in them the past, present, and future are seen. Nor is there any reason for anyone to wonder at this, for the <term>star</term> <note>original: *sidus vel astrum*; refers to the celestial influence or the "sidereal body" that governs the inner life.</note> or constellation, through its influence, produces the image and likeness of the matter in question within the crystal. This must indeed occur through the compulsion of the constellation, as is prefigured in <term>Magic</term> <note>original: *Magia*; referring to "Natural Magic," the study of the hidden virtues and signatures within nature.</note>; and in this way, the star descends most powerfully into its subject—

<summary>This page explains that the heavens feel compassion for human suffering and send omens before death. It introduces "Necromancy" as a natural branch of astronomy and describes how stellar influences allow practitioners to see the past, present, and future within crystals and beryls.</summary>

<keywords>Necromancy, Astronomy, beryls, crystal gazing, stellar influence, compassion of the stars, Natural Magic, omens</keywords>

--- Page 130 ---
<meta>Continuity: This page concludes the sentence from page 95 regarding how the "star" or celestial influence descends into a physical medium. It moves from the general mechanism of stellar influence to the role of human faith and imagination in "ruling the stars," specifically through scrying techniques.</meta>

<page-num>96</page-num>
<header>THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS</header>

—subjected, <note>Completing the word from the previous page, likely *subjectum*.</note> just as a ray of the Sun is directed into a crystal. All things that nature contains are known to the stars, and since the stars are subject to humans, those things can be compelled into a subject through the stars, so that they fulfill the human will; and according to the practitioner's knowledge, the operation achieves its effect. 

Regarding faith, it is said that it will be most efficacious, which is true in itself, just as Christ speaks of faith and what it performs: faith is nothing other than a certain **power**, which works through the word, and the word raises the dead. What else is found in the stars, other than that through faith they are overcome by nature? And just as through faith in Christ a mountain is moved into the sea by a word, so also in **natural faith** it must be known and understood that through words the stars are drawn down from the heavens. These stars then demonstrate their operations according to our <term>imagination</term> <note>original: *imaginationem*; in Paracelsian thought, this is not "make-believe," but a focused mental force capable of shaping reality and directing celestial energy.</note>; for the **wise man rules the stars**, <note>original: *sapiens dominatur astris*; a famous astrological maxim of the era, suggesting that while the stars incline the soul, the enlightened mind is not a slave to them.</note> that is, the wise man who is skilled in the art of compelling such powers into obedience to himself. 

In this way, certain visions perceptible to the eye are imprinted in **mirrors, beryls, waters, fingernails,** <note>original: *vnguibus*; referring to 'onychomancy,' the ancient practice of divining by observing reflections on polished or oiled fingernails.</note> and similar objects, according to the power of the practitioner and the unity of their mental conception.

<meta>The catchword at the bottom of the original page is "Eue," likely leading into "Euentus" (events) or a similar word on page 97.</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus explains that stars can be compelled by human will and faith to reveal hidden knowledge. He equates the power of "natural faith" with religious miracles, arguing that a wise practitioner uses their imagination to direct stellar influences into scrying tools like crystals and mirrors.</summary>

<meta>Continuity: This page continues the discussion from page 96 regarding how the "wise man" interacts with the stars. While the previous page focused on the theoretical "natural faith" required to perceive celestial influence, this page provides concrete examples of how stellar "direction" manifests as unexpected luck or fate in daily life, such as finding treasure or a spouse.</meta>

### THE SCIENCE OF ASTRONOMY 97

It also happens at times that something prosperous or unlucky occurs unexpectedly through the **direction of the stars**, just as when someone finds something by surprise without having sought it. For example, if someone bought a house and, while building, found a **hidden treasure** within it, this happens by the favor of the stars with which they follow him. 

In another case, where a traveler coming from two hundred miles away arrives at a place where he finds and gains a wife according to his desire, he was led there by his **star**. Many things happen thus by surprise, which are understood to be under **Fate**, and their operations ought to be known by the **Astronomer** <note>In this historical context, an "Astronomer" refers to a practitioner of judicial astrology—one who interprets how the movements of the heavens influence human destiny.</note>, so that he may be able to point out when and where a similar thing might happen; thus, the fortunate path may be followed and the contrary avoided. 

This art was once held in the highest esteem among the **ancient wise men**; but those who eat and devour the dead <note>This is a characteristic polemic of the author (likely Paracelsus), mocking traditional scholars or physicians who rely on "dead" books and anatomical study of corpses rather than the "living" light of nature.</note> have rejected it. In its place, they have offered only their own **black work** to the world, and they have forbidden anyone to learn or look into anything other than their **Pharisaic leaven** <note>original: *pharisaicum fermentum*; a biblical allusion to Matthew 16:6, used here to describe the corrupting, hypocritical doctrines of the established academic or religious authorities.</note>. And when, after the death of men, certain souls and spirits have sometimes appeared, [freeing] them...

<summary>This page explains how the "direction of the stars" governs unexpected events like finding treasure or a spouse, framing these as manifestations of Fate that a skilled Astronomer should interpret. The author concludes with a sharp critique of contemporary scholars, accusing them of suppressing this ancient wisdom in favor of their own corrupt "leaven."</summary>

<keywords>astrology, direction of the stars, fate, hidden treasure, marriage, ancient wise men, Pharisaic leaven, Astronomer, Paracelsus</keywords>

<meta>Continuity: This page continues from the discussion on page 97 regarding the "souls and spirits" that appear after death. Having criticized traditional scholars for ignoring these phenomena, Paracelsus now explains his physiological and spiritual theory of the "two bodies"—one physical (elementary) and one astral (sidereal)—to account for the appearance of ghosts and specters.</meta>

### THEOPHR. PARAC. 98

...they have rarely attempted: truly, understand what such spirits have been, just as was also said before: that a human being has a **sidereal spirit or body** <note>Also known as the "astral body," this is a subtle form composed of the "light of nature" or star-stuff, distinct from the physical flesh and the eternal soul.</note>, and along with it an **elementary body** <note>The physical body made of the four elements (earth, water, air, fire), which returns to the earth upon death.</note>, so that there are two bodies in a single human being. 

When a person dies and is buried, the body that has its origin from the **elements** must decay; for this to happen, a certain amount of time is required, though it is longer for one person than for another. The **sidereal body** must also decay and be consumed, just like the elementary one. It does not remain within the dead body, but lingers near it, around it, or travels to those places where the person lived or which they frequently visited. It remains in those places until the time of its consumption is finished; once that time ends, the body is consumed along with that spirit. 

This **sidereal body** was formerly mistaken for the person themselves, because the properties of this kind of body were unknown in the past. For there are various spirits: some are seen and some are not; others are heard and some are not. Sometimes they appear by day, and sometimes by night, etc. In whatever way they are manifested, they are nothing other than a **celestial operation** <note>original: *operatio coelestis*; Paracelsus argues that ghosts are not the actual souls of the dead, but the lingering "star-body" still being acted upon by celestial forces.</note> in those sidereal bodies, just as...

<meta>catchword: above</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus introduces his dual-body theory, explaining that humans possess both a physical "elementary" body and a "sidereal" or astral body. He argues that ghosts are actually the decaying remains of the sidereal body, which lingers in familiar places after death until it is eventually consumed.</summary>

<keywords>sidereal body, astral body, elementary body, death, decay, spirits, ghosts, celestial operation, Paracelsus, anatomy of the spirit</keywords>

### THE KNOWLEDGE OF ASTRONOMY <note>original: "S C I E N T. A S T R O N O M." abbreviated for "Scientia Astronomica"</note>

...as was stated above, and it is useful for no other thing than for consumption <note>Refers to the final breakdown of the astral body after it has finished its earthly purpose.</note>. More broadly, it should be noted that certain other phantasms <note>original: "phantasmata"; here Paracelsus refers to beings that appear physical but lack the eternal soul given to the descendants of Adam.</note> appear, which are thought to be men, but there is no one who knows their origin. These include **Giants**, **Nymphs**, **Gnomes**, and **domestic Pygmies**; these four types <note>original: "generationes"</note> of beings are wonderful. It is only given to men to know this: that these beings do not derive their origin from us. For they are not descended from **Adam** <note>The biblical first man; Paracelsus believed these beings were a separate creation of God, existing between the spirit world and the human world.</note>, but are indeed another kind of people with properties far different from our own.

For instance, **Giants** once walked upon the earth, yet their beginning and end were not human, because no one has seen them [be born or die]. Nevertheless, they stayed among men, moved about, ate, and so on; just as they had come, so they departed. **Nymphs** <note>Elemental spirits of the water.</note> have dwelt in the waters and upon them. **Gnomes** <note>Elemental spirits of the earth.</note> built their dwellings under the mountains and in their caverns, as well as arched chambers <note>original: "testudines"; literally "tortoises," referring here to vaulted or domed ceilings.</note> with a height of about a cubit <note>A cubit is an ancient unit of measurement, roughly 18 inches or the length of a forearm.</note>, more or less. **Domestic Pygmies** <note>Also called "house-servants" or kobolds; spirits that dwell within human homes.</note> existed and served men, and they performed wonderful works; yet no one ever saw their origin or their end.

But we shall bring forward as much as can be judged from philosophy <note>In this period, "philosophy" refers to the study of natural laws and the hidden workings of the universe.</note>. Man and the star <note>original: "astrum"; the celestial influence or "heaven" that shapes physical life.</note> together generate a man, as from the generations...

<summary>This page introduces the four classes of elemental beings—Giants, Nymphs, Gnomes, and Pygmies—explaining that while they interact with humans, they are not descendants of Adam and have a non-human origin and nature.</summary>

## 100

->THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS<-

...can be understood of men, namely in this way: that they are made from an **elemental** and **sidereal mass** <note>original: "elementari, & syderea massa"; the material and astral substance that makes up the universe.</note>, yet destined through men, who are also made from that same mass. It follows from this that a conception can occur from the mass beyond man and without him, through the forces of influences; for the stars <note>original: "sydera"</note> compress and also conceive. In these matters there are various impregnations, and therefore also various conceptions and diverse generations. 

For the **imaginative power** <note>original: "virtus imaginativa"; Paracelsus believed the "imagination" of the heavens or the soul could physically shape matter.</note> of the influence generates without a human, and from that union a certain mass is forged, which passes into a form according to how that same image prevailed in its star. By this reasoning, various animals are generated which were not present in the first creation <note>Referring to the six days of Genesis.</note>, but their mass was indeed in the earth, which was prepared by the constellations for that which was contained within it. 

So also there were masses in the firmament which, through the operations of the **Stars**, erupted into **Giants**, and some into **Nymphs**, others into **Gnomes**, or into **Pygmies**; these indeed perished together with the said constellations. We, however, are all from **Adam**, and we are those who ought to be called men <note>Paracelsus draws a sharp line here: while elemental beings are biological and "astral" realities, they lack the specific spiritual lineage of Adam which defines a "true" human being.</note>.

<meta>catchword: ought (debent)</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus explains that elemental beings like Giants and Nymphs are created by the "imaginative power" of the stars acting upon physical matter, independent of human reproduction. He distinguishes these "astral" beings from true humans, who descend from Adam.</summary>

## 101

->SCIENTIFIC ASTRONOMY 101<-

...they ought <note>original: "debent"; continuing the thought from the previous page that true humans "ought to be called men."</note>; likewise, God—the Father of all and our Father—made our first parent <note>referring to Adam</note> with His own hands, without the influence of the stars: such is the generation of man.

Another point is again worthy of consideration: namely, that the sons of Adam did not occupy the whole world, and for this reason several parts of it are hidden from us. These areas are inhabited not by us, but by other creatures—beings existing as humans created outside the creation of <term>Adam: the first human in the biblical tradition, from whom the "soul-bearing" human lineage descends.</term> For God wished to leave no vacuum, but to fill the world with other wonders apart from us humans, as is explained more extensively regarding generations, etc.

This, however, is very clearly known to us: that the four generations mentioned before—humans, or rather <term>semi-humans: beings like nymphs or gnomes that resemble humans but, according to Paracelsus, lack an immortal soul.</term>—existed as predestined by the influence of the heavens alone, and through a mass and <term>seed: the "sperma" or formative power that acts as a "blueprint" for a substance or being.</term> organized by itself, within those places which they inhabit. These are humans without a <note>original: "anima"</note> soul, and in whom there is nothing eternal: unless perhaps the merciful and almighty God should infuse a soul into them, contrary to the nature of <term>Adamite: those belonging to the human bloodline of Adam, distinguished by possessing a spiritual and immortal nature.</term> humans; this is within His power. Similarly, it can be most certainly proven that those who dwell in remote and unknown regions of the world [possess] souls...

<summary>Paracelsus distinguishes between "Adamite" humans, created directly by God with immortal souls, and "semi-humans" or elementals, who are generated through astral influence and lack an eternal nature unless granted one by divine miracle.</summary>

...to have <note>original: "habere" (inferred from the catchword "rum" on page 101 and the suffix "bere" here); continuing the discussion of whether remote peoples possess souls.</note>, for they arose after the flood <note>The Great Flood of Noah.</note>, perhaps even without a soul, unless God, through marriage with humans who possess a soul, should wish to endow them with the same.

<header>ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCE</header>

...of Gnomes, Giants, etc., concerning whom it is doubted whether they possess a human soul. ### PROOF CONCERNING THE SCIENCE
->Signed.<- <note>Refers to "Scientia Signata," the Doctrine of Signatures, which suggests that the outward appearance of a person or object reveals its inner spiritual essence.</note>

Whatever is produced by nature acquires the form of its own **virtue** <term>virtue: the inherent power or essential spiritual quality of a substance or being.</term>. For example, just as the mind, properties, and nature of a man might be, nature herself grants the form of the body to him, according to the common proverb: *The uglier the man, the more perverse he is found to be.* **Adam** was perfectly formed in soul and body, so that there was nothing deformed in him; but once he knew good and evil, nature then gained the power to delineate every individual according to their goodness or malice. For before Adam possessed this kind of knowledge, he was pleasing to God, but after him, [punishment/penitence]...

<summary>This page explains that elemental beings like Gnomes can be manipulated by conjurors through their 'sidereal bodies.' It also introduces the Doctrine of Signatures, arguing that physical appearance is a direct reflection of a person's inner morality, a process that began after Adam's fall.</summary>

--- Page 142 ---
<meta>Continuity: Page 104 continues the discussion from page 103 regarding the "Science of Signatures." Having established that Adam’s fall allowed nature to mark human bodies according to their inner character, Paracelsus now explains that man is subject to the laws of nature in the same way plants are. The text focuses on how the "heart" acts as a seed that determines the outward form of the body.</meta>

<page-num>104</page-num>
<header>THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS</header>

...established, namely that God made man; and so man was placed into the power of nature, where she works with him no differently than she does with the flowers of the field: she marks these so that they may become known to everyone. Similarly, man is marked so that he may be known forever, just like all things that nature has produced. And since nothing can be so hidden in man that it cannot be revealed, know that this very thing happens in three ways: by the **signs of nature**, by **one's own betrayal** <note>original: "proditione propria"; Paracelsus refers here to "self-betrayal," where a person's true nature is revealed through their involuntary actions, speech, or slips of the tongue.</note>, and by the **judgment of God**. Leaving the latter two aside, I shall take up the task of explaining the first: the indication of the signs implanted by nature. 

Just as you see every seed hidden in the earth brought into the light above the earth by nature, so that its secret is revealed and everyone may now see what and what kind of seed had been sown in that place; likewise, the heart of man is its own **seed** <note>original: "semen"; in this context, the heart is viewed as the spiritual "germ" or blueprint from which the physical body grows.</note>, out of which nature expresses the body, so that everyone may know what kind of heart it was.

And although this can be understood more clearly and more extensively in flowers than in man, nevertheless the **art** <term>art: specifically the Science of Signatures, the practice of reading internal character through external physical traits.</term> itself teaches it expressly. While living here on earth, we investigate whatever lies hidden beneath the mountains and we examine it by outward signs and similarities. In a like manner, a[ll]...

<meta>Catchword: ll [from "omnes"]</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus argues that man is subject to nature's laws just like plants; nature "marks" the human body to reveal the internal "heart," which acts as a seed for our physical form. He identifies three ways secrets are revealed—nature’s signs, self-betrayal, and divine judgment—focusing here on nature's signs.</summary>

<page-num>105</page-num>
# ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCE 105

<meta>Catchword "dīju-" at bottom right.</meta>

...[All] properties of herbs and stones <note>original: "omnes herbarum proprietates, lapidum"</note> we recognize through their **signed mark** <note>original: "per suum signum signatum"; in this philosophy, the 'signum' is the outward hint—like the shape of a leaf—that reveals the 'signatum,' the inner medicinal or spiritual essence.</note>. Also, in any person, nothing can exist or be hidden that is not outwardly marked in them. And just as the Physician has knowledge in his art, so also the **Astronomer** <note>original: "Astronomus"; for Paracelsus, an astronomer studied the "inner stars" or spiritual forces within a person, not just the planets in the sky.</note> has knowledge in the thing marked.

There are three things through which nature makes man manifest, along with whatever is produced by her:

First is **Chiromancy** <note>original: "Chiromantia"</note>, which holds the primary and outward parts in man, such as the hands, feet, and other parts of the body, the lines, the wrinkles, etc., all of which come together at once. 

The second is **Physiognomy** <note>original: "Physiognomia"</note>; this concerns the form of the face and everything that the entire head contains. 

The third is **constitution** <note>original: "habitus"; referring to the overall physical build, posture, and outward appearance of the whole body.</note>, which determines the proportion of the whole body. These three flow together, and through them any creature can be known by men: just as medicine is known by the Physician, man by the Astronomer, and metal by the miner <note>original: "mineralium fossore"</note>; so it is in the remaining arts, where the subject is known by any **Artist** <term>Artist: the skilled practitioner or "Alchemist" who understands the hidden workings of nature.</term>. 

This Art is the mother from which the understandings of all things are learned (according to the "what" and "of what kind" <note>original: "secundum quid & quale"</note>, as the **Logicians** <note>original: "Dialectici"; the academic philosophers of the time who focused on categorization.</note> speak). And whoever is ignorant of this art, and the secrets [to be] judged <note>original: "dijudicare"; completion of the catchword "dīju-"</note> from the three things which have been said...

<summary>This page explains that nature reveals the inner essence of all things—plants, stones, and humans—through outward signatures. It identifies Chiromancy, Physiognomy, and overall body constitution as the three tools used by "Artists" like physicians and astronomers to decode these secrets.</summary>

<keywords>Science of Signatures, Chiromancy, Physiognomy, Habitus, Paracelsian Astronomy, medical Artist, Signatum, Dialectics</keywords>

<header>THEOPHR. PARAC.</header>

...[to] judge <note>original: "dijudicare"; this completes the catchword "diju-" from page 105.</note> at all, he can in no way be a **natural artist** <term>naturalis artifex: a skilled practitioner who works with the living forces of nature rather than just book learning.</term>, nor a natural physician, nor a philosopher who is able to know the mysteries of nature, or speak even the least bit about them.

From these things the foundation is laid: since all things have their own seed, which in turn are contained within this seed; and nature is the **craftswoman of forms** <note>original: "natura fabricatrix"; a central idea that nature acts as a creative artisan, shaping the physical appearance of things to match their inner purpose.</note>, which she indicates within themselves according to the essences of things, as well as the conditions and properties of that form. For essences are invisible, except through these three <note>Referring to Chiromancy, Physiognomy, and Habitus—the three tools of observation described on the previous page.</note>. For this reason, they are highly necessary in **Astronomy** <note>original: "Astronomia"; for Paracelsus, this is the study of how celestial and spiritual forces manifest in physical matter.</note>, and because they were ordained and created at the moment of conception. Therefore, the explanation and judgment of natural things comes from Astronomy, along with everything else that is born from that **Horoscope** <note>original: "Horoscopo"; here used to mean the specific structural "blueprint" or "rising" of a thing's nature at its origin.</note>.

### CONCLUSION

Therefore, it has seemed to me quite sufficient to write down, declare, and prove Astronomy with all its species or parts, and not to omit it: especially because among the ancients it was once held in such high esteem and value that no one except a **King** <note>original: "Rex"</note> or a **High Priest** <note>original: "summus Sacerdos"</note> might learn it, because of its dignity and the altogether marvelous works which [proceed...]

**T**hese things have been said by me so that the worldly wisdom which now thrives in this century should not be puffed up, but rather, being humbled, might learn **Astronomy** <term>Astronomia: Not just the mapping of planets, but the study of the "internal heaven" and how celestial forces influence human life.</term>, which was never weaker at any time before than in this very age of ours. It is highly necessary, and without it, a man should by no reason be called a man. For it teaches us to recognize beauty and honor; it can point out the heavens to men, as well as the meanings of the stars in the works of God, regarding both the first and the second heaven. <note>In Paracelsian thought, the "first heaven" is the physical sky, while the "second heaven" refers to the spiritual or inner firmament within man.</note> By this art, the **spirit of giddiness** <term>spiritus vertiginis: A biblical term (Isaiah 19:14) referring to a spirit of confusion, error, or spiritual blindness sent as a judgment.</term> is identified, and the evil is discerned from the good—which I consider the greatest thing of all. # ASTRONOMICAL SCIENCE

->(h 2)<-

...they are preserved from deceptions. Through it [Astronomy], the end of all arts can be learned, such as **medicine**, **metals**, **herbs**, **mechanics**, etc., as well as the knowledge of the soul innate to humans. From the beginning of birth, anyone can be judged through Astronomy, so that they may be known internally by their **influence** <term>influentia: In the Paracelsian view, this is the celestial "flow" of power from the stars into the human body and soul, shaping a person's character and health.</term>, and from this influence—if it be evil—they can be withdrawn; if good, they can be educated and guided according to it. This ensures they are not led by the whim of a **spirit of giddiness** <term>spiritus vertiginis: A term derived from the Bible (Isaiah 19:14) referring to a spirit of confusion or spiritual blindness that causes people to stagger in their judgment and go astray.</term>, but by the way of truth. 

For all these things I have sufficiently proven Astronomy, and I have set forth its powers and virtues. The **seven books** <note>Paracelsus frequently organized his major treatises into seven-book structures; here he refers to the following practical volumes of his astronomical system.</note> which follow, and which contain the practice of universal Astronomy, will indicate more broadly how each individual species ought to be led particularly into its own perfect judgment. This is done so that through those seven books the entire **power and virtue of the firmament** <note>original: "firmamenti vis atq; virtus"</note> may be made open and come into use for men. 

And this is especially fitting for **Great Lords and the Leaders of the World** <note>original: "Magnatibus & Capitibus Mundi"</note> even more than for the common people, so that many things which are done poorly can be both forbidden and avoided, and good things can be sustained, which otherwise perish utterly through ignorance of this same art. Since, however, the **prophecy of Christ** <note>A likely reference to the biblical "signs in the sun, moon, and stars" or the promise that all hidden things will be revealed.</note> is so near, one must consider one thing...

Paracelsus claims that by understanding celestial influences, leaders can guide people toward truth and away from spiritual confusion, noting that his subsequent seven books will explain these powers in detail.</summary>

### FROM THE PHILOSOPHY OF PARACELSUS
### concerning the Prophet Isaiah,
### a fragment.

An **Augurist** <term>augurista: A practitioner of augury, traditionally one who interprets omens from the flight of birds, but here used by Paracelsus to describe anyone claiming personal prophetic power.</term> is truly defined as one who makes himself a foreteller and sets forth signs from which he attempts to predict the future however he can—through divinations, dreams, clouds, **Geomancy** <term>geomantia: Divination using markings on the ground or patterns formed by tossed handfuls of soil, rocks, or sand.</term>, **Hydromancy** <term>hydromantia: The art of divining the future or the unknown by observing water.</term>, **Chaomancy** <term>chaomantia: In Paracelsian thought, this is divination through the "Chaos" or the atmosphere/air, often involving the observation of visions or shapes in the sky.</term>, birds, and so forth. The foundation of these arts consists of spirits, who direct this business according to their own will, leading either toward truth or toward a lie. Such men were held in the highest esteem by the common people, just as the Philistines had countless numbers of them, whom they even believed to be from heaven; otherwise, they did not think it possible that

# THE KNOWLEDGE OF ASTRONOMY, BOOK III

...that they might know similar things: and from this it followed that their accumulation <note>referring to the "heaping up" of occult errors or false knowledge mentioned in the fragment</note> was greatly increased. Augury <term>Augury: traditionally the interpretation of omens, but used here by Paracelsus as a broad term for various types of divination.</term> contains within itself a specific kind that teaches how to inspect the fingernails of the thumbs or the hands, as well as beryls <note>original: *berillos*. Clear gemstones or crystals used as "show-stones" for scrying or seeing visions.</note>, and so on, according to their specific preparations. For this business, the Jews did not employ their own sons, because they considered them unsuitable, but rather the sons of the Augurists, whom they believed to be closer to God.

Through such auguries, they discovered treasures—gold and silver in the streams and mountains—so that their regions were filled with gold and silver. Just as even now in this time, many are found who, through the auguries of rods <note>referring to dowsing or divining rods used to find ore</note>, beryls, and the like, discover minerals and treasures. They reach these things through the guidance of spirits and the work of demons, primarily so that men might forget their God—which is the main reason they <insert>the demons</insert> perform these deeds. 

The Jews regarded these Augurists as gods, and after their death, they erected statues to them in their memory, believing that the souls and spirits of these men dwelt within the statues as if they were still living. In place of those spirits, however, the devil entered, and so on. This still tends to happen today:

->when men withdraw from God, so that they<-
->approach these things: namely, that<-
->they fall into idolatry.<-

->THE END<-

<summary>In this concluding fragment, Paracelsus describes how the ancient Jews were led into idolatry through "augury," specifically using crystals and divining rods to find gold. He warns that these successes are actually demonic traps designed to make humanity forget God.</summary>

*Pseudo-astrologers wrongly and falsely attribute vices to the stars, page 10, line 14.*
*When man wishes to know more than nature is capable of in physical matters, he is seduced by the Devil, page 11, line 10.*

### INDEX

God does not want men to learn the most false arts of sorcery <term>sortilegiorum: the practice of divination or magic to influence or predict events</term>, but rather his commandments, page 12, line 9.

Why spirits indicate doubtful and fabricated things to men rather than truths, same page, line 9.

A twofold ascendant <note>original: *Duplex ascendens*. This refers back to the concept of influences on a person's nature—one from spirits and one from the stars.</note>: namely the spirit which [teaches] pyromancy <term>Pyromantiam: a form of divination involving the observation of fire or flames</term>, and the star which teaches astronomy, same page, line 26.

The devil introduces a twofold idolatry into pyromancy, page 14, line 10.

The Greeks, more than other nations, are liars and inventors of lying arts, page 15, line 6.

The less a man trusts in God, the greater a sorcerer he can become, page 16, line 4.

The devil mixes superstitions—such as prayers, conjurations, etc.—into sorceries only so that wretched mortals might think they compel spirits through God, when God wishes it not at all, page 17, line 20.

Natural auguries <term>Auguria: the interpretation of signs and omens, often from the behavior of animals or natural phenomena</term> are permitted, but after spirits mingle themselves into these, they are the ruin of the body, honor, health, and all goods of those using them, as well as a presumption of vain wisdom, page 18, line 14.

The five senses of brute animals far surpass the senses of men, page 19, line 13.

Presages <note>original: *Præfagia*. Natural signs that foretell future events or changes, such as weather.</note> are to be sought from nature alone, page 20, line 6.

Any animals are greatest at foretelling in their own regions.

# INDEX

*True philosophy teaches that there are only two elements, namely the hot and the cold*, page 54, line 13. <note>In traditional Aristotelian thought, there are four elements (earth, air, fire, water), but this author simplifies them into two primary qualities.</note>

*What an element truly is for physicists* <term>Physicis: here referring to natural philosophers, those who study the laws of the physical world</term>, page 55, line 27.

*The heavens consist of two elements, the male; and the other two elements are the earth, his female*, page 56, line 20. <note>This reflects an early modern alchemical view where the cosmos is divided into active/masculine celestial forces and passive/feminine terrestrial forces.</note>

*Heat is produced from the heavens, and coldness from the globe* <note>original: "globulo", referring to the terrestrial sphere or the Earth.</note>, page 57, line 1.

*The five types of astronomy*, page 60, line 6.

*Definitions of the types of astronomy*, page 61, line 19.

*Subdivisions of the types of astronomy*, page 62, line 21.

*Definitions of all the aforementioned types resulting from the five species of astronomy*, page 63, line 23.

*The star in the firmament is that which rules over all things, and in which all animal wisdom exists* <note>original: "brutalis... sapientia". This refers to the instinctive knowledge or "wisdom" possessed by animals, which was believed to be governed by the stars.</note>, page 66, line 20.

*Man was created from a mass gathered by the Lord God into One out of all creatures*, page 70, line 11.

*All creatures were created from nothing, but man was created from one body* <note>The author distinguishes between the "ex nihilo" (out of nothing) creation of the universe and the composite creation of man, who contains the essence of all other things.</note>, same page, line 14.

*Man is the fifth being of all creatures and of the greater world; therefore, he is called the lesser world* <term>minor mundus: the Microcosm, the idea that the human being is a miniature map of the entire universe</term>, page 70, line 23.

*Because man is made of the four elements and one star, he is called the fifth essence* <term>essentia quinta: the Quintessence, a refined substance beyond the four physical elements</term>, page 71, line 6.

*Man, the machine of the world, having been made and transmuted into flesh and blood*, same page, line 18.

<meta>catchword: Homo (Man)</meta>

<summary>This index page outlines a cosmology where the universe is governed by hot and cold elements and celestial "male/female" dynamics. It culminates in the definition of Man as the "Microcosm" or Quintessence, a unique being synthesized from all elements and stars.</summary>
